&\hskip 15.0pt\times\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\Bigl[\Bigl(\Re\bigl[{\mathrm{i}}e_{\ell^{\prime}}(x)\bigr],\Im\bigl[{\mathrm{i}}e_{\ell^{\prime}}(x)\bigr]\Bigr)\cdot\nabla_{\Re[\widehat{r}^{\ell^{\prime}}],\Im[\widehat{r}^{\ell^{\prime}}]}\Bigl(\prod_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\rho\bigl(\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr)\Bigr)\Bigr]\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}\bigl[{\mathrm{i}}e_{k}(x)\bigr]\bigl(m_{t}*f_{N}\bigr)(x)dx\biggr],\end{split}

where

\epsilon_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}},{\mathfrak{P}}}=1
except if

(\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}},\mathfrak{P})=(\Re,\Im)
in which case

\epsilon_{\Re,\Im}=-1
(those choices following from the standard relationships

\Re[zz^{\prime}]=\Re[z]\Re[z^{\prime}]-\Im[z]\Im[z^{\prime}]

and

\Im[zz^{\prime}]=\Re[z]\Im[z^{\prime}]+\Im[z]\Re[z^{\prime}]
, for
z,z^{\prime}\in{\mathbb{C}}
,
which explains why only

\epsilon_{\Re,\Im}
is here equal to
-1
), and
with

\begin{split}&B_{k,\ell}^{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}},\mathfrak{P}}(t)=-\epsilon_{\mathfrak{P}}4\pi\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}k\cdot D{\mathcal{H}}^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}(t,m_{t})(x)\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}\bigl[{\mathrm{i}}e_{k}(x)\bigr]\mathfrak{P}\Bigl[\widehat{f}^{\ell^{\prime}}_{N}e_{-\ell^{\prime}}(x)\Bigr]\,dx,\end{split}

where
\epsilon_{\mathfrak{P}}=1
if
\mathfrak{P}=\Re
and
-1
if
\mathfrak{P}=\Im
.

We now recall the following standard fact from the theory of ODEs. If we let

J_{t}=\textrm{\rm det}\biggl(\Bigl(\partial_{\mathfrak{P}[\widehat{m}_{0}^{k^{\prime}}]}\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}[\widehat{m}_{t}^{k}]\Bigr)_{k,k^{\prime}\in F_{N}^{+},\mathfrak{P},\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}=\Re,\Im}\biggr),

then

(74)

\frac{d}{dt}J_{t}=J_{t}\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigl(-4\pi|k|^{2}+A_{k,k}^{\Re,\Re}(t)+A_{k,k}^{\Im,\Im}(t)+B_{k,k}^{\Re,\Re}(t)+B_{k,k}^{\Im,\Im}(t)\Bigr).

Third Step.
The goal is now to prove that the sum in
the right-hand side of
(
74
) is lower bounded, by a constant that is independent of

\varepsilon

and

\rho
.
The easiest term to handle is the sum

\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}[B_{k,k}^{\Re,\Re}(t)+B_{k,k}^{\Im,\Im}(t)]
.
Indeed, in the definition of

B_{k,\ell}^{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}},\mathfrak{P}}(t)
,

D{\mathcal{H}}^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}
is bounded independently
of
(N,\varepsilon,\rho)
,
since

V_{1}
and
V_{2}
are

d_{-2}
-Lipschitz continuous, see
(
63
) and Proposition

4.10
.

The term
\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}[A_{k,k}^{\Re,\Re}(t)+A_{k,k}^{\Im,\Im}(t)]
is much more difficult to handle. We get

\begin{split}&A_{k,k}^{{\mathfrak{P}},\mathfrak{P}}(t)=4\pi^{2}(1-\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}d\lambda\biggl(\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\widehat{r}^{j}\biggr)\\
&\hskip 7.0pt\times\biggl[\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in F_{N}^{+}}k\cdot\bigl(\partial^{2}_{pp}H(\lambda,t)\ell^{\prime}\bigr)\,\partial_{\mathfrak{P}[\widehat{m}^{k}]}V_{2-\lambda}\bigl(t,m_{t}^{N,\varepsilon}(r)*f_{N}\bigr)\hat{f}^{k}_{N}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\times\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\Bigl[\Bigl(\Re\bigl[{\mathrm{i}}e_{\ell^{\prime}}(x)\bigr],\Im\bigl[{\mathrm{i}}e_{\ell^{\prime}}(x)\bigr]\Bigr)\cdot\nabla_{\Re[\widehat{r}^{\ell^{\prime}}],\Im[\widehat{r}^{\ell^{\prime}}]}\Bigl(\prod_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\rho\bigl(\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr)\Bigr)\Bigr]{\mathfrak{P}}\bigl[{\mathrm{i}}e_{k}(x)\bigr]\bigl(m_{t}*f_{N}\bigr)(x)dx\biggr],\end{split}

which, by integration by parts, can be rewritten as

\begin{split}&A_{k,k}^{{\mathfrak{P}},\mathfrak{P}}(t)=-4\pi^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}d\lambda\biggl(\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\widehat{r}^{j}\biggr)\\
&\hskip 7.0pt\times\biggl[\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in F_{N}^{+}}k\cdot\bigl(\partial^{2}_{pp}H(\lambda,t)\ell^{\prime}\bigr)\,V_{2-\lambda}\bigl(t,m_{t}^{N,\varepsilon}(r)*f_{N}\bigr)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\times\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}=\Re,\Im}\Bigl[{\mathfrak{P}}\bigl[{\mathrm{i}}e_{k}(x)\bigr]\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}\bigl[{\mathrm{i}}e_{\ell^{\prime}}(x)\bigr]\partial^{2}_{\mathfrak{P}[\widehat{r}^{k}],\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}[\widehat{r}^{\ell^{\prime}}]}\Bigl(\prod_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\rho\bigl(\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr)\Bigr)\Bigr]\bigl(m_{t}*f_{N}\bigr)(x)dx\biggr],\end{split}

and then,

\begin{split}&\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+},\mathfrak{P}=\Re,\Im}A_{k,k}^{{\mathfrak{P}},\mathfrak{P}}(t)=-4\pi^{2}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}d\lambda\biggl(\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\widehat{r}^{j}\biggr)\biggl[V_{2-\lambda}\bigl(t,m_{t}^{N,\varepsilon}(r)*f_{N}\bigr)\\
&\hskip 50.0pt\times\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}=\Re,\Im}\sum_{k,\ell\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigl[\Bigl({\mathfrak{P}}\bigl[{\mathrm{i}}e_{k}(x)\bigr]k\Bigr)\cdot\Bigl(\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}\bigl[{\mathrm{i}}e_{\ell}(x)\bigr]\partial^{2}_{pp}H(\lambda,t)\ell\Bigr)\partial^{2}_{\mathfrak{P}[\widehat{r}^{k}],\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}[\widehat{r}^{\ell}]}\Bigl(\prod_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\rho\bigl(\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr)\Bigr)\Bigr]\\
&\hskip 50.0pt\times\bigl(m_{t}*f_{N}\bigr)(x)dx\biggr].\end{split}

We can now apply
Corollary

3.18
since
V_{2-\lambda}
is displacement semi-concave. Using the fact that
H
is convex with respect to
p
, we deduce that

\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+},\mathfrak{P}=\Re,\Im}A_{k,k}^{{\mathfrak{P}},\mathfrak{P}}(t)\geq-C_{N},\quad t\in[0,T],

for a constant
C_{N}
depending on
N
, but independent of
\rho
and
\varepsilon
, from which we deduce that

J_{t}\geq-C_{N}
, for any
t\in[0,T]
.
The result follows from a mere change of variable at any time
t
, using the lower bound for the Jacobian
J_{t}
.
∎

5.
Application to Mean Field Games

Following our agenda, we now address weak solutions to the conservative form of the master equation
(
23
).

5.1.
Notion of weak solution

We start with the following definition:

Definition 5.1
.

We call a weak solution
to the master equation a bounded measurable function

{\mathcal{Z}}:[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
,
such that
the system of Fourier coefficients

(75)

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}:[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\ni(t,m)\mapsto\hbox{\hskip 441.65944pt\hskip-23.14009pt\hbox{$\Bigl\{{\mathcal{Z}}(t,m,\cdot)\Bigr\}$}\hskip-23.14009pt\hskip-441.65944pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{52312104}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-441.65944pt\hskip 441.65944pt}^{-k},\quad k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d},

satisfies

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{0}\equiv 0
together with
the following two properties:

(i)

There exists a sequence of functions

(\zeta_{N}:[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}})_{N\geq 1}
,
such that, for any
N\geq 1
,
for
\mathfrak{P}=\Re,\Im
,
the following equation holds true in the distributional sense on
[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
:

(76)

\begin{split}&\epsilon_{\mathfrak{P}}\partial_{t}\mathfrak{P}\bigl[\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}(t,m)\bigr]-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{\mathfrak{P}[\widehat{m}^{k}]}\biggl\{\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}H\biggl(y,{\mathrm{i}}2\pi\sum_{j\in F_{N}}j\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{j}(t,m)e_{j}(y)\biggr)dm(y)\biggr\}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j\in F_{N}}2\pi^{2}|j|^{2}\partial_{\mathfrak{P}[\widehat{m}^{k}]}\Bigl(\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{j}(t,m)\widehat{m}^{j}\Bigr)+\mathfrak{P}\biggl[\hbox{\hskip 473.61424pt\hskip-26.301pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\frac{\delta F}{\delta that items (2) and (3) are redundant, as the combination of (1) and (3) implies (2).

Proof.

The proof relies on the following main observation: for a given integer
N_{0}\geq 1
,

{\mathcal{P}}_{N_{0}}
is of zero measure under

{\mathbb{P}}_{N}
(or equivalently when
it is regarded as a subset of

{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
equipped with the image of the Lebesgue measure on

{\mathcal{O}}_{N}
by the canonical mapping

{\mathscr{I}}_{N}
in
(
28
)); this follows from the fact that the elements of

{\mathcal{P}}_{N_{0}}
are required to have zero Fourier coefficients of modes
k
with
|k|\geq N_{0}
.
Therefore, we can define
{\mathcal{V}}
inductively, by first assigning values on
[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{1}\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
, and then on
[0,T]\times({\mathcal{P}}_{2}\setminus{\mathcal{P}}_{1})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
, so on and so forth…
To make it clear, there is no difficulty for defining
{\mathcal{V}}
such that,
for every
N\geq 1
,
for every
t\in[0,T]
,
for almost every
m
in

{\mathcal{P}}_{N}

and
for any
x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
,

(80)

{\mathcal{V}}(t,m,x)=\sum_{k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}}\biggl\{\hbox{\hskip 476.73471pt\hskip-30.44681pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\frac{\delta V}{\delta m}(t,m)(\cdot)$}\hskip-30.44681pt\hskip-476.73471pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{56952404}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-476.73471pt\hskip 476.73471pt}^{k}e_{-k}(x)\biggr\}=\sum_{k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}}\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k}}V(t,m)e_{k}(x).

We then let

\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{k}(t,m):=\hbox{\hskip 476.73471pt\hskip-21.33453pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathcal{V}}(t,m)(\cdot)$}\hskip-21.33453pt\hskip-476.73471pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{56952404}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-476.73471pt\hskip 476.73471pt}^{-k}(=\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k}}V(t,m))

and then

\begin{split}2\zeta_{N}(t,m)&:=\partial_{t}V(t,m)-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}H\biggl(y,{\mathrm{i}}2\pi\sum_{k\in F_{N}}k\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{k}(t,m)e_{k}(y)\biggr)dm(y)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\sum_{k\in F_{N}}2\pi^{2}|k|^{2}\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{k}(t,m)\widehat{m}^{k}+F(m)\\
&=\partial_{t}V(t,m)-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}H\biggl(y,{\mathrm{i}}2\pi\sum_{k\in F_{N}}k\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k}}V(t,m)e_{k}(y)\biggr)dm(y)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\sum_{k\in F_{N}}2\pi^{2}|k|^{2}\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k}}V(t,m)\widehat{m}^{k}+F(m),\end{split}

which satisfies
(
77
) thanks to
(
36
).

Multiplying, for each value of

k\in F_{N}^{+}
,
both sides of the definition of

\zeta_{N}(t,m)
above
by
(1/2)\partial_{\mathfrak{P}[\widehat{m}^{k}]}\varphi(t,(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}})

for an arbitrary smooth test function on
[0,T]\times{\mathcal{O}}_{N}

with a support included in
(0,T)\times{\mathcal{O}}_{N}
, we
easily get
(
76
) (in a distributional sense). Indeed, it suffices to observe that

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}\partial_{t}V\bigl(t,m\bigr)\partial_{\mathfrak{P}[\widehat{m}^{k}]}\varphi\Bigl(t,(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\Bigl(\Re\bigl[\widehat{m}^{j}\bigr],\Im\bigl[\widehat{m}^{j}\bigr]\Bigr)\\
&=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}\partial_{\mathfrak{P}[\widehat{m}^{k}]}V\bigl(t,m\bigr)\partial_{t}\varphi\Bigl(t,(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\Bigl(\Re\bigl[\widehat{m}^{j}\bigr],\Im\bigl[\widehat{m}^{j}\bigr]\Bigr)\\
&=\epsilon_{\mathfrak{P}}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}\mathfrak{P}\Bigl[{\mathcal{V}}^{k}\bigl(t,m\bigr)\Bigr]\partial_{t}\varphi\Bigl(t,(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\Bigl(\Re\bigl[\widehat{m}^{j}\bigr],\Im\bigl[\widehat{m}^{j}\bigr]\Bigr),\end{split}

with

m
in the above three lines being understood
as
m={\mathscr{I}}_{N}((\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}})

and
with the last line following from
(
30
). Similarly,

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}F\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\partial_{\mathfrak{P}[\widehat{m}^{k}]}\varphi\Bigl(t,(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\Bigl(\Re\bigl[\widehat{m}^{j}\bigr],\Im\bigl[\widehat{m}^{j}\bigr]\Bigr)\\
&=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}\partial_{\mathfrak{P}[\widehat{m}^{k}]}F\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\varphi\Bigl(t,(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\Bigl(\Re\bigl[\widehat{m}^{j}\bigr],\Im\bigl[\widehat{m}^{j}\bigr]\Bigr)\\
&=-\int_{0}^{T}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}\mathfrak{P}\biggl[\hbox{\hskip 476.73471pt\hskip-23.801pt\hbox{$\displaystyle\frac{\delta F}{\delta m}(m,\cdot)$}\hskip-23.801pt\hskip-476.73471pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{56952404}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-476.73471pt\hskip 476.73471pt}^{k}\biggr]\varphi\Bigl(t,(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\Bigl(\Re\bigl[\widehat{m}^{j}\bigr],\Im\bigl[\widehat{m}^{j}\bigr]\Bigr).\end{split}

This proves
(i)
in Definition

5.1
. As for
(ii)
in the same definition,
it follows from an obvious integration by parts.

We prove that the values of
{\mathcal{V}}
that are hence defined are bounded by a common constant.
In order to do so, we use the fact that
V
is Lipschitz continuous (in space)
for the
d_{-2}
-distance and thus for the
d_{\textrm{\rm TV}}
-distance. Indeed,
Proposition

4.10
says that there exists a constant
C
such that, for any
N\geq 1
,

\textrm{\rm essup}_{(t,m)\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\biggl|\partial_{x}\biggl(\sum_{k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}}\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k}}V(t,m)e_{k}(x)\biggr)\biggr|\leq C.

By
(
80
),
this proves that

(81)

\sup_{N\geq 1}\textrm{\rm essup}_{(t,m)\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}}\textrm{\rm essup}_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\bigl|\partial_{x}{\mathcal{V}}(t,m,x)\bigr|\leq C.

Since
{\mathcal{V}}(t,m,\cdot)
has zero mean, this yields

(82)

\sup_{N\geq 1}\textrm{\rm essup}_{(t,m)\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}}\textrm{\rm essup}_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\bigl|{\mathcal{V}}(t,m,x)\bigr|\leq C.

It then remains to extend

{\mathcal{V}}
to the whole

[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}

by taking a weak-star limit, in the
\sigma^{*}(L^{\infty};L^{1})
sense, of the bounded sequence

([0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\ni(t,m,x)\mapsto{\mathcal{V}}(t,m*f_{N},x))_{N\geq 1}
.
Weak limits are defined almost everywhere under
\textrm{\rm Leb}_{1}\otimes{\mathbb{P}}\otimes\textrm{\rm Leb}_{d}
. Since
\cup_{N\geq 1}{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
is of zero measure under

{\mathbb{P}}
(the argument is given next), we can easily modify
any weak limit such that it coincides with

{\mathcal{V}}
itself
on

[0,T]\times(\cup_{N\geq 1}{\mathcal{P}}_{N})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
: the weak limit then provides an extension of
{\mathcal{V}}
to the whole

[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
, which shows (1).
The main difficulty is to prove that there is in fact only one limit point.
In order to do so, we proceed as follows.
For any subsequence
(N_{n})_{n\geq 1}
for which
the sequence

([0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\ni(t,m,x)\mapsto{\mathcal{V}}(t,m*f_{N_{n}},x))_{n\geq 1}

is converging in the weak sense,
we can apply
Lemma

5.7

below
with
{\mathcal{W}}(m*f_{N},x)={\mathcal{V}}(t,m*f_{N},x)
and
W^{(N)}(m)=V(t,m)

and then invoke Lemma

5.8

with
{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{W}}_{2}
being two possible
limiting points
of

({\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\ni(t,m,x)\mapsto{\mathcal{V}}(t,m*f_{N},x))_{N\geq 1}

and with
W_{1}=W_{2}=V
.
The key point in this respect is that
the sequence

([0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\ni(t,m)\mapsto V(t,m*f_{N}))_{N\geq 1}

converges to
the function

[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\ni(t,m)\mapsto V(t,m)
.
Therefore,
Lemma
5.8
says that the sequence

([0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\ni(t,m,x)\mapsto{\mathcal{V}}(t,m*f_{N},x))_{N\geq 1}

has a unique limit point (in the weak sense)
and is thus convergent (in the weak sense).
This proves (2) in the statement.

It then remains to prove that
each
{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
is of zero measure under
{\mathbb{P}}
.
It suffices to
recall from
Theorem

3.5

that, for
N^{\prime}>N
,

\begin{split}{\mathbb{P}}\bigl({\mathcal{P}}_{N}\bigr)&=\bigl({\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{N^{\prime}}}\cdot{\mathbb{P}}\bigr)\Bigl({\mathcal{P}}_{N}\Bigr)=\bigl({\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{N^{\prime}}}\cdot{\mathbb{P}}\bigr)\Bigl(\bigcap_{k\in F_{N^{\prime}}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\bigl\{m:[\pi_{N^{\prime}}^{(2)}(m)]^{k}=0\bigr\}\Bigr)=0.\end{split}

This completes the proof.
∎

Importantly, we stress (once again) the fact that the function
{\mathcal{V}}
defined in the above statement
inherits the
d_{-2}
-Lipschitz property and the semi-concavity of
V
.
By Proposition

4.10
and by
(
80
),
we have (see
(
81
)
for the first line below)

\begin{split}&\sup_{N\geq 1}\textrm{\rm essup}_{(t,m)\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\bigl|\partial_{x}{\mathcal{V}}(t,m,x)\bigr|<\infty,\\
&\sup_{N\geq 1}\textrm{\rm essup}_{(t,m)\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}}\sum_{k\in F_{N}}|k|^{4}|\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{k}(t,m)|^{2}<\infty.\end{split}

Moreover,
we recall from Corollary

3.18

(see in particular (
34
))
that, with

\rho
as in Definition

3.13
,
for any
N\geq 1
, for any
(t,m)\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

with
m
having a strictly positive density,
for any collection of complex numbers
(z^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}
and any non-negative symmetric matrix
S
,

(83)

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}\biggl\{V\Bigl(t,m^{N,\varepsilon}(r)*f_{N}\Bigr)\\
&\hskip 45.0pt\times\sum_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}=\Re,\Im}\sum_{k,\ell\in F_{N}^{+}}\biggl[\Bigl({\mathfrak{P}}\bigl[z^{k}\bigr]k\Bigr)\cdot\Bigl(\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}\bigl[z^{\ell}\bigr]S\ell\Bigr)\partial_{\mathfrak{P}[\widehat{r}^{k}],\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}[\widehat{r}^{\ell}]}^{2}\Bigl(\prod_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\rho\bigl(\Re\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr],\Im\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr]\bigr)\Bigr)\biggr]\biggr\}\\
&\hskip 30.0pt\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\Bigl(\Re\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr],\Im\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr]\Bigr)\\
&\leq C|S|\sum_{q=1}^{d}\biggl(\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}|k_{q}|\,|z^{k}|\biggr)^{2}.\end{split}

with
C
depending on
c
such that
m\geq 1/c
.
By integration by parts,
this can be rewritten

(84)

\begin{split}&-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}\biggl\{\sum_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}=\Re,\Im}\sum_{k,\ell\in F_{N}^{+}}\biggl[\partial_{\mathfrak{P}[\widehat{r}^{k}]}V\Bigl(t,m^{N,\varepsilon}(r)*f_{N}\Bigr)\widehat{f}_{N}^{k}\\
&\hskip 45.0pt\times\biggl[\Bigl({\mathfrak{P}}\bigl[z^{k}\bigr]k\Bigr)\cdot\Bigl(\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}\bigl[z^{\ell}\bigr]S\ell\Bigr)\partial_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}[\widehat{r}^{\ell}]}\Bigl(\prod_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\rho\bigl(\Re\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr],\Im\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr]\bigr)\Bigr)\biggr]\biggr\}\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\Bigl(\Re\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr],\Im\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr]\Bigr)\\
&\leq C|S|\sum_{q=1}^{d}\biggl(\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}|k_{q}|\,|z^{k}|\biggr)^{2},\end{split}

and then

(85)

\begin{split}&-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}\biggl\{\sum_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}=\Re,\Im}\sum_{k,\ell\in F_{N}^{+}}\biggl[\mathfrak{P}\Bigl[\widehat{\mathcal{V}}^{k}\Bigl(t,m^{N,\varepsilon}(r)*f_{N}\Bigr)\Bigr]\widehat{f}_{N}^{k}\\
&\hskip 40.0pt\times\biggl[\Bigl({\mathfrak{P}}\bigl[z^{k}\bigr]k\Bigr)\cdot\Bigl(\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}\bigl[z^{\ell}\bigr]S\ell\Bigr)\partial_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}[\widehat{r}^{\ell}]}\Bigl(\prod_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\rho\bigl(\Re\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr],\Im\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr]\bigr)\Bigr)\biggr]\biggr\}\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\Bigl(\Re\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr],\Im\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr]\Bigr)\\
&\leq C|S|\sum_{q=1}^{d}\biggl(\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}|k_{q}|\,|z^{k}|\biggr)^{2},\end{split}

which prompts us to introduce the following definition:

Definition 5.3
.

A bounded measurable function

{\mathcal{Z}}:[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

is said to be one-sided Lipschitz in the weak sense
if, for any
c>1
,
there exists a constant
C
such that, for any
N\geq 1
,
any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)

and any

\rho
as in Definition

3.13
,
it holds, for any
(t,m)\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})

with
m
having a density lower bounded by
1/c
,
and
for any collection of complex numbers
(z^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}
and any symmetric matrix
S
,

\begin{split}&-\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}\biggl\{\sum_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}=\Re,\Im}\sum_{k,\ell\in F_{N}^{+}}\biggl[\mathfrak{P}\Bigl[\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}\Bigl(t,m^{N,\varepsilon}(r)*f_{N}\Bigr)\Bigr]\widehat{f}_{N}^{k}\\
&\hskip 45.0pt\times\biggl[\Bigl({\mathfrak{P}}\bigl[z^{k}\bigr]k\Bigr)\cdot\Bigl(\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}\bigl[z^{\ell}\bigr]S\ell\Bigr)\partial_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{P}}[\widehat{r}^{\ell}]}\Bigl(\prod_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\rho\bigl(\Re\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr],\Im\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr]\bigr)\Bigr)\biggr]\biggr\}\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\Bigl(\Re\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr],\Im\bigl[\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr]\Bigr)\\
&\leq C|S|\sum_{q=1}^{d}\biggl(\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}|k_{q}|\,|z^{k}|\biggr)^{2},\end{split}

with
\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}(t,m)=\hbox{\hskip 473.61424pt\hskip-18.14008pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathcal{Z}}(t,m,\cdot)$}\hskip-18.14008pt\hskip-473.61424pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{56500484}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-473.61424pt\hskip 473.61424pt}^{-k}
.

5.2.
Uniqueness of weak solutions

Here is now the main theorem:

Theorem 5.4
.

Let
{\mathbb{P}}
be the probability measure on
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
introduced in the statement of Theorem

3.5
. Then,
uniqueness holds everywhere on
[0,T]
,

{\mathbb{P}}
-almost everywhere
on
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
and
everywhere on
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}

within the class of
bounded measurable functions

{\mathcal{Z}}:[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}},

satisfying (with the same notation as in
(
75
))

(1)

\displaystyle\sup_{N\geq 1}\textrm{\rm essup}_{(t,m)\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}}\biggl[\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\Bigl|\sum_{k\in F_{N}}k\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}(t,m)e_{k}(x)\Bigr|+\sum_{k\in F_{N}}|k|^{4}|\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}(t,m)|^{2}\biggr]<\infty;

(2)

{\mathcal{Z}}
is one-sided Lipschitz in the weak sense, see
Definition

5.3
;

(3)

for any

k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
,
for any
t\in[0,T]
,
for any bounded (measurable)
function
\varphi
defined on
{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
, for some
N_{0}\geq 1
,

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}(t,m*f_{N})\varphi\bigl((\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)=\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}(t,m)\varphi\bigl((\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m).

In particular,
for every
t\in[0,T]
, for

{\mathbb{P}}
almost every
m\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and
for every
x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
,

{\mathcal{Z}}(t,x,m)
is
equal to

{\mathcal{V}}(t,x,m)
,
with

{\mathcal{V}}
being as in the statement of
Proposition
5.2
.

The following remarks are in order.

Remark 5.5
.

Item
(1)
in the statement of Theorem

5.4

says that

\sup_{N\geq 1}\textrm{\rm essup}_{(t,m)\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}}\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\Bigl|\partial_{x}\Bigl(\sum_{k\in F_{N}}\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}(t,m)e_{k}(x)\Bigr)\Bigr|<\infty.

Thus,
for any
N\geq 1
and for almost every
(t,m)\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
,
the function

x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\mapsto\sum_{k\in F_{N}}\mathcal{Z}^{k}(t,m)e_{k}(x)

is Lipschitz
continuous,
with a Lipschitz constant that is independent of
N
,
t
and
m
.

Remark 5.6
.

It is worth noticing that the formulation of uniqueness depends on the choice of the measure
{\mathbb{P}}
in the statement of Theorem

3.5
.
This is an intriguing observation since our choice for the measure
{\mathbb{P}}

is somewhat arbitrary,
recall the definition
(
27
) of the approximating measure
{\mathbb{P}}_{N}
.
Alternatively, in order to formulate uniqueness
without imposing a specific choice for

{\mathbb{P}}
,
we could also wonder about identifying

\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}(t,m)
for
m\in{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
, for a fixed
N
, but this looks
rather challenging.
In fact, with our approach and with item
(iii)
in
Theorem
3.5
, we
identify

\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}(t,m*f_{N})\varphi\bigl((\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)

up to a remainder that tends to
0
as
N
tends to
\infty
.
This remainder depends on

\varphi
but can be made uniform when
\varphi
is taken in a compact family of continuous
functions.
Interestingly, the above integral can be reformulated as an integral on

{\mathcal{O}}_{N}
equipped with the Lebesgue measure,
which makes the interpretation easier.

Proof.

First Step.

We start with an arbitrary weak solution, as given in
Definition
5.1
.
We observe
from (
76
)
that, for any
N\geq 1
,
the functions
(\Re[\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}],\Im[\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}])_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}
satisfy a system of
hyperbolic
equations of the form

\begin{split}&\epsilon_{\mathfrak{P}}\partial_{t}\mathfrak{P}\Bigl[\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}(t,m)\Bigr]+\partial_{\mathfrak{P}[\widehat{m}^{k}]}H_{N}\Bigl(t,m,\Bigl(\Re\bigl[\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{j}(t,m)\bigr],\Im\bigl[\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{j}(t,m)\bigr]\Bigr)_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)=0,\end{split}

for
\mathfrak{P}\in\{\Re,\Im\}
,
k\in F_{N}^{+}
and
(t,m)\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
,
with
{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
being identified with

{\mathcal{O}}_{N}
and with

\epsilon_{\Re}=1
and
\epsilon_{\Im}=-1
.

Following the proof of Theorem 6.6 in
[
28
]
(which is itself adapted from

[
51
]
),
we deduce that, for almost every
t\in[0,T]
, we can find a function

U^{(N)}(t,\cdot):{\mathcal{P}}_{N}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
such that, almost everywhere on
{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
,

\epsilon_{\mathfrak{P}}\mathfrak{P}\Bigl[\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}(t,m)\Bigr]=\partial_{\mathfrak{P}[\widehat{m}^{k}]}U^{(N)}(t,m),\quad k\in F_{N}^{+},

which can be easily reformulated by means of
(
30
) as

\hbox{\hskip 451.29065pt\hskip-23.14009pt\hbox{$\Bigl\{{\mathcal{Z}}(t,m,\cdot)\Bigr\}$}\hskip-23.14009pt\hskip-451.29065pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{53617400}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14236pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-157.14285pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-451.29065pt\hskip 451.29065pt}^{-k}=\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}(t,m)=\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k}}U^{(N)}(t,m).

Then, the function
U^{N}
can be easily assumed to measurable in time (assuming for instance that

t\mapsto U^{N}(t,m_{0})
is measurable for a fixed
m_{0}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{N}
).
Following the same mollification procedure as in the second and third steps of
the proof of
[
28
, Theorem 6.6]
and expanding the form of
H_{N}
, we can even assume that

U^{(N)}
is Lipschitz in time and space on
[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
and satisfies, almost everywhere, the following equation

\begin{split}&\partial_{t}U^{(N)}(t,m)-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}H\biggl(y,{\mathrm{i}}2\pi\sum_{k\in F_{N}}k\partial_{\widehat{m}_{k}}U^{(N)}(t,m)e_{k}(y)\biggr)dm(y)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\sum_{k\in F_{N}}2\pi^{2}|k|^{2}\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k}}U^{(N)}(t,m)\widehat{m}^{k}+F(m)+2\zeta_{N}(t,m)=0,\end{split}

with
U^{(N)}(T,m)=G(m)
as boundary condition.
Intuitively,
the above equation is obtained by taking the anti-derivative in (
78
).

Second Step.

For the same value of
N
,
and for

\varepsilon
and
\rho
and with the same notation as in Definition
3.13
,
we now consider the function

\widetilde{U}^{(N,\varepsilon,\rho)}:m\in{\mathcal{P}}_{N}\mapsto\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}U^{(N)}\bigl(t,m^{N,\varepsilon}(r)\bigr)\prod_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\rho\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}\bigr)\bigotimes_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}d\widehat{r}^{k}.

Pay attention that

\widetilde{U}^{(N,\varepsilon,\rho)}

is not the same as

(U^{(N)})^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}
in Definition

3.13

and in
Corollary
3.16
, since the
measure argument in
U^{(N)}
is not convoluted by
f_{N}
.

For a given
t\in[0,T]
,
\widetilde{U}^{(N,\varepsilon,\rho)}
is continuously differentiable with respect to

(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{N}
. Moreover, for almost every
t\in[0,T]
, for any two
m_{1},m_{2}\in{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
, we have

\begin{split}&U^{(N,\varepsilon,\rho)}\bigl(t,m_{2}\bigr)-U^{(N,\varepsilon,\rho)}\bigl(t,m_{1}\bigr)\\
&=\int_{0}^{1}\biggl[\sum_{k\in F_{N}}\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k}}U^{(N,\varepsilon,\rho)}\bigl(t,\lambda m_{2}+(1-\lambda)m_{1}\bigr)\bigl(\widehat{m}_{2}^{k}-\widehat{m}_{1}^{k}\bigr)\biggr]d\lambda\\
&=(1-\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}\biggl\{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}\biggl[\sum_{k\in F_{N}}\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k}}U^{(N)}\Bigl(t,\lambda m_{2}^{N,\varepsilon}(r)+(1-\lambda)m_{1}^{N,\varepsilon}(r)\Bigr)\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}_{2}-\widehat{m}_{1}^{k}\bigr)\biggr]\\
&\hskip 200.0pt\times\prod_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\rho\bigl(\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\widehat{r}^{j}\biggr\}d\lambda\\
&=(1-\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{1}\biggl\{\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}\biggl[\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\Bigl[\sum_{k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}}\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{k}\Bigl(t,\lambda m_{2}^{N,\varepsilon}(r)+(1-\lambda)m_{1}^{N,\varepsilon}(r)\Bigr)e_{k}(x)\Bigr]d\bigl(m_{2}-m_{1}\bigr)(x)\biggr]\\
&\hskip 200.0pt\times\prod_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\rho\bigl(\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\widehat{r}^{j}\biggr\}d\lambda.\end{split}

By means of Remark

5.5
,
we know that,
for almost every
(t,m)\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
,
the function
x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\mapsto\sum_{k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}}\widehat{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{k}(t,m)e_{k}(x)
is Lipschitz continuous (in
x
),
uniformly in
(t,m)
and in
N\geq 1
.
This shows that
the Lipschitz constant of
\widetilde{U}^{(N,\varepsilon,\rho)}(t,\cdot)
in
m\in{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
with respect to
the distance
d_{W_{1}}

is uniform with respect to
N\geq 1
and almost every
t\in[0,T]
.
Letting
\rho
converge to the Dirac mass
\delta_{0}
and then

\varepsilon
tend to
0
, we deduce that
the same is true for
U^{(N)}
: its Lipschitz constant in
m\in{\mathcal{P}}_{N}

with respect to
d_{W_{1}}
is uniform in
N\geq 1
and
t\in[0,T]
(since

U^{(N)}
is already known to be continuous, it is quite straightforward to pass from
almost every
t\in[0,T]
to any
t\in[0,T]
).

Moreover,
by
using the growth properties of the Hamiltonian and by using
item (1) in the statement of
Theorem

5.4
together with (
77
), we deduce that
the essential supremum norm of

\partial_{t}U^{(N)}

is bounded, uniformly with respect to
t
and
N
, on
any
subset of
{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
of the
form
\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}_{N}:\|m\|_{\infty}\leq c\}
, for any
c>1
.
In particular, using the fact that

U^{(N)}(T,\cdot)=G(\cdot)
, we deduce that, for any
c>1
,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\bigl|U^{(N)}(t,m)\bigr|{\mathbf{1}}_{\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}_{N}:\|m\|_{\infty}\leq c\}}d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)

is bounded by a constant independent of
N
. For
c>1
, the
subset

\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}_{N}:\|m\|_{\infty}\leq c\}
is of positive measure under
{\mathbb{P}}_{N}

(as it contains a neighborhood of the Lebesgue measure),
from which we deduce that

\sup_{N\geq 1}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\inf_{m\in{\mathcal{P}}_{N}}\bigl|U^{(N)}(t,m)\bigr|<\infty.

Using the Lipschitz property of
U^{(N)}
in space, we get

(86)

\sup_{N\geq 1}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sup_{m\in{\mathcal{P}}_{N}}\bigl|U^{(N)}(t,m)\bigr|<\infty.

Third Step.

By assumption (1) in the statement,
we observe that

U^{(N)}
satisfies the two main conclusions of Proposition

4.10
,
for a fixed value of
N
therein and for almost every
(t,m)\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
.
Similarly, using the weak
one-sided Lipschitz property of
{\mathcal{Z}}
in
Definition
5.3

and reverting the computations
in (
83
)–(
84
)–(
85
),
we can easily deduce that

U^{(N)}
satisfies the conclusions of Corollary

3.18
, say in the form of
(
34
), again for a fixed value of
N
.
Importantly, this suffices to
repeat the arguments underpinning the proof of
Theorem

4.12
.
Notice indeed that, for a given value of
N
, the fact that

U^{(N)}
is defined on
[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
suffices to
give a meaning to
(U^{(N)})^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}

in Definition

3.13

and then to follow the computations of
Proposition

4.11

and
Theorem
4.12

when
N
therein is fixed.
The key fact
is that, by combining the assumption
(
77
)
with
Theorem
6.15
(which permit to handle the remainder
\zeta_{N}
along the
characteristics
(
65
)),
we still have
(
70
), namely
for an
initial
m_{0}\in B_{N}(c)={\mathcal{P}}_{N}\cap\{m:\sup_{x\in\mathbb{T}^{d}}|\nabla m(x)|\leq c,\ \inf_{x\in\mathbb{T}^{d}}m(x)\geq 1/c\}
for a constant
c>1
,

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}\Bigl|\bigl(V^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}-(U^{(N)})^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}\bigr)\bigl(0,m_{0}(r)\bigr)\Bigr|\prod_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\rho_{0}\bigl(\widehat{r}^{j}\bigr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\widehat{r}^{j}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\leq\eta_{N,\varepsilon}+C_{N,\rho_{0}}\int_{0}^{T}\biggl[\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}R^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}\bigl(t,(\widehat{y}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{\{y\in{\mathcal{P}}_{N}\}}\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\widehat{y}^{j}\biggr]dt,\end{split}

with

\lim_{(N,\varepsilon)\rightarrow(\infty,0)}\eta_{N,\varepsilon}=0
,
with

C_{N,\rho_{0}}
depending on
N
,

\rho_{0}
and
c
, but independent of
\varepsilon
and
\rho
,
and with the remainder

R^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}
being
as in the statement of Proposition

4.11
.

Letting
\rho
tend to the Dirac mass at
0
for a fixed value of
N
and invoking
Proposition
4.11
, we can get rid of the term containing

R^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}
in the above inequality.
Using the regularity of
V
and
U^{(N)}
with respect to

d_{W_{1}}
, we deduce that there exists a sequence

(\eta_{N}^{\prime})_{N\geq 1}
, converging to
0
, such that

|V(0,m_{0})-U^{(N)}(0,m_{0})|\leq\eta_{N}^{\prime}
when
m_{0}\in B_{N}(c)
.
Replacing
m_{0}
by
m*f_{N}
,
we deduce that

(V(0,m*f_{N})-U^{(N)}(0,m*f_{N}))_{N\geq 1}
tends to
0
for any
m\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
with a continuously differentiable strictly positive density.
Recalling from
(i)
in Theorem

3.5

that, for
{\mathbb{P}}
almost every

m
,

m

has a continuously differentiable strictly positive density, we deduce that,
for
{\mathbb{P}}
almost every

m
,

(V(0,m*f_{N})-U^{(N)}(0,m*f_{N}))_{N\geq 1}

tends to
0
. Obviously, the same holds true for any initial time
t\in[0,T]
instead of
0
.

We eventually deduce that, for any
t\in[0,T]
, the sequence

(m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\mapsto U^{(N)}(t,m*f_{N}))_{N\geq 1}

converges
to
V(t,\cdot)

for the weak-star topology
\sigma^{*}(L^{\infty}({\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}),{\mathbb{P}});L^{1}({\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}),{\mathbb{P}}))
.
The next step is to apply
Lemma

5.8

below
to
({\mathcal{W}}_{1},W_{1})=({\mathcal{Z}},V)
and

({\mathcal{W}}_{2},W_{2})=({\mathcal{V}},V)
, from which we deduce that

{\mathcal{Z}}
and
{\mathcal{V}}
are equal.
The key point is indeed to observe from assumption (3) in the statement that, for any
t\in[0,T]
,
for any
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
,
the sequence

(m\mapsto\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}(t,m*f_{N}))_{N\geq 1}

converges
to

m\mapsto\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{k}(t,m)

for the weak-star topology
\sigma^{*}(L^{\infty}({\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}),{\mathbb{P}});L^{1}({\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}),{\mathbb{P}}))
. In this respect,
the form of assumption (3) is sufficient to identify uniquely
any weak limit: this follows from the
description of the Borel
\sigma
-algebra
on
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
provided by Lemma

3.4
together with a monotone class argument.
We deduce that, for any
t\in[0,T]
, for
{\mathbb{P}}
almost every

m\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
, the two functions

{\mathcal{V}}(t,m,\cdot)

and

{\mathcal{Z}}(t,m,\cdot)

coincide.
∎

5.3.
Auxiliary lemmas

We now provide two important auxiliary lemmas that we invoked in the proofs
of
Proposition

5.2

and
Theorem

5.4
.

Lemma 5.7
.

Let
{\mathcal{W}}:{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\times{\mathbb{T}}^{d}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

and

W:{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

be bounded measurable functions such that
for a collection of bounded functions
(W^{(N)}:{\mathcal{P}}_{N}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}})_{N\geq 1}
, with each
W^{(N)}
being Lipschitz continuous, the following three assumptions hold true:

(i)
For any
N\geq 1
, any
k\in F_{N}^{+}
and almost every

m\in{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
,

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{k}(m)=\partial_{\widehat{m}_{k}}W^{(N)}(m)
,
with
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{k}(m):=\hbox{\hskip 473.61424pt\hskip-16.19565pt\hbox{$\displaystyle{\mathcal{W}}(m,\cdot)$}\hskip-16.19565pt\hskip-473.61424pt\raisebox{18.50012pt}{\hbox{$\hbox{\raisebox{-3.34055pt}{\resizebox{56500484}{141084}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\mathchoice{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-853.45891pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{10.00012pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\scriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.4pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.98pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}{\raisebox{-165.94817pt}{\resizebox{468892385}{36044800}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.94444pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathchoice{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\displaystyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{1.0pt}{$\textstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.7pt}{$\scriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}{\raisebox{0.0pt}{\resizebox{0}{0}{\hbox{\raisebox{0.5pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\kern-0.6pt\bigwedge\kern-0.6pt$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}}}}$}}\hskip-473.61424pt\hskip 473.61424pt}^{-k}
;

(ii)
Up to a common subsequence,
the functions

(m\mapsto\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{k}(m*f_{N}))_{N\geq 1}

converge,
for each
k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
,
to

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{k}

for the weak-star topology
\sigma^{*}(L^{\infty}({\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}),{\mathbb{P}});L^{1}({\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}),{\mathbb{P}}))
;

(iii)

Up to the same subsequence as in
(ii)
,
the
functions

(m\mapsto W^{(N)}(m*f_{N}))_{N\geq 1}

converge
to
W

for the same weak-star topology
\sigma^{*}(L^{\infty}({\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}),{\mathbb{P}});L^{1}({\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}),{\mathbb{P}}))
.

Then, for any
\delta>0
and
c>1
, there exists
an integer
N_{c,\delta}
such that, for any
N_{0}\geq N_{c,\delta}
,
any
k_{0}\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}
, any smooth function
\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

whose support is included in

\{(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}:1+2\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re[\widehat{m}^{j}e_{-j}(x)]\geq 1/c\}
,

\begin{split}&\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}W(m)\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{\mathcal{P}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\biggl\{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{k_{0}}(m)-2W(m)|k_{0}|^{2pd}\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}\biggr\}\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\biggr|\leq C\delta,\end{split}

for a constant
C
independent of
N_{0}
and
depending on
\varphi
only through

\|\varphi\|_{\infty}
and

\|\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\varphi\|_{\infty}
.

Notice that the role of
N_{0}
in the statement is to
adjust the size of the support of the test function

\varphi
.

Proof.

Throughout
the proof, we fix
\delta>0
and
c>1
and then choose

N_{0}\geq 1
,
\vartheta:[0,T]\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

and
\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
as in the statement.
Without any loss of generality, we also do as if the subsequences in assumptions
(ii)
and

(iii)
were the full sequence
(N)_{N\geq 1}
itself.

By item
(iii)
in the statement, we get

\begin{split}\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}W^{(N)}\bigl(m*f_{N}\bigr)\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)&=\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}W(m)\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m).\end{split}

Using the fact that the functions

(W^{(N)})_{N\geq 1}
are uniformly bounded
to get the first equality and
invoking

(iii)
in
the statement of
Theorem

3.5
, the above limit can be reformulated as

(87)

\begin{split}&\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}W^{(N)}\bigl(m*f_{N}\bigr)\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)=\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}W(m)\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m).\end{split}

Here, we can easily replace

\varphi
by its partial derivative

\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\varphi
,
for a given
k_{0}\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}
,

\begin{split}&\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}W^{(N)}\bigl(m*f_{N}\bigr)\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt=\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}W(m)\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m).\end{split}

We now recall the form of

{\mathbb{P}}_{N}
in
(
27
)–(
28
) in order to study the left-hand side.
We have

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}W^{(N)}\bigl(m*f_{N}\bigr)\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&=\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}W^{(N)}\bigl(m*f_{N}\bigr)\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\Bigl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\Bigr)\bigotimes_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{k},\end{split}

with
p\geq 5

fixed
(see (
27
) for the original occurrence of
p
)
and with
m
in the second line being obviously identified with

{\mathscr{I}}_{N}((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}})
.

Fix now
N\geq 1
. By Lemmas

6.3

and

6.5
in the appendix, we can choose
N_{0}

large enough such that,
if

N\geq N_{0}
and the support of
\varphi
is included in
{\mathscr{I}}_{N}^{-1}(A_{N_{0}})
(with
A_{N_{0}}
as in the statement of
Lemma
6.3
),
then

(88)

{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\biggl(\Bigl\{ (\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\in\textrm{\rm support}(\varphi)\Bigr\}\cap\biggl\{\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|\widehat{m}^{k}|\geq\frac{a_{0}}{|k|^{5d/2}}\biggr\}\biggr)\leq\delta,

for
a_{0}
fixed as in the statement of Lemma

6.3
(in particular,
a_{0}
only depends on the dimension). By the same statement, the simple fact that

{\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}})\in A_{N_{0}}\quad\text{and}\quad\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|\widehat{m}^{k}|<\frac{a_{0}}{|k|^{5d/2}}

implies
that
(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{N}
.
For such a choice, we get that

\begin{split}&\biggl|\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}W^{(N)}\bigl(m*f_{N}\bigr)\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}W^{(N)}\bigl(m*f_{N}\bigr)\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\\
&\hskip 50.0pt\times{\mathbf{1}}_{\{\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|\widehat{m}^{k}|<a_{0}/|k|^{5d/2}\}}\exp\Bigl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\Bigr)\bigotimes_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{k}\biggr|\leq C\delta,\end{split}

with
C
depending on

\varphi
through

\|\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\varphi\|_{\infty}
, but being independent of
N
.

Then, we can make an integration by parts in the second term in the left-hand side.
This leads to two terms:

(a)

one derivative is acting on
W^{(N)}
and leads to

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{k_{0}}
(using item
(i)
in the statement);
(b)
the other derivative is acting on the density.
Therefore, we obtain

\begin{split}&\biggl|\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}W^{(N)}\bigl(m*f_{N}\bigr)\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}\biggl\{\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}W^{(N)}\bigl(m*f_{N}\bigr)\widehat{f}_{N}^{k_{0}}-2W^{(N)}\bigl(m*f_{N}\bigr)|k_{0}|^{2pd}\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}\biggr\}\\
&\hskip 30.0pt\times{\mathbf{1}}_{\{\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|\widehat{m}^{k}|<a_{0}/|k|^{5d/2}\}}\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\Bigl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\Bigr)\bigotimes_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{k}\biggr|\\
&\leq C\delta,\end{split}

and then,
using once
again
(
88
) together with the
bound

\sup_{N\geq 1}\textrm{\rm essup}_{m\in{\mathcal{P}}_{N}}|\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}W^{(N)}(m)|=\sup_{N\geq 1}\textrm{\rm essup}_{m\in{\mathcal{P}}_{N}}|\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{k_{0}}(m)|<\infty
,

\begin{split}&\biggl|\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}W^{(N)}\bigl(m*f_{N}\bigr)\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\biggl\{\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}W^{(N)}\Bigl(m*f_{N}\Bigr)\widehat{f}_{N}^{k_{0}}-2W^{(N)}\bigl(m*f_{N}\bigr)|k_{0}|^{2pd}\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}\biggr\}\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\biggr|\\
&\leq C\delta,\end{split}

with
C
now depending on
\varphi
through
\|\varphi\|_{\infty}

and

\|\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\varphi\|_{\infty}
.
Recalling again that

\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}W^{(N)}(m*f_{N})=\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{k_{0}}(m*f_{N})

almost everywhere, we can use assumption
(ii)
in the statement in order to pass to the limit in the term

\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}W^{(N)}(m*f_{N})
.
Recalling
(
87
)
and letting
N
tend to
\infty
, we eventually get

\begin{split}&\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}W(m)\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{\mathcal{P}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\biggl\{\widehat{\mathcal{W}}^{k_{0}}(m)-2W(m)|k_{0}|^{2pd}\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}\biggr\}\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\biggr|\leq C\delta,\end{split}

which completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 5.8
.

Let

({\mathcal{W}}_{1},W_{1})
and

({\mathcal{W}}_{2},W_{2})
be two pairs of functions
satisfying the assumptions of Lemma

5.7
.
If
W_{1}
and
W_{2}
are equal almost everywhere under
{\mathbb{P}}
, then

{\mathcal{W}}_{1}
and
{\mathcal{W}}_{2}

are also equal
almost everywhere under
{\mathbb{P}}
.

Proof.

We apply
Lemma
5.7
to
({\mathcal{W}}_{i},W_{i})
, for
i=1,2
, and then use the fact that
W_{1}=W_{2}
.
We deduce that,
for
\delta>0
and
c>1
, there exists
an integer
N_{c,\delta}
such that, for
N_{0}\geq N_{c,\delta}
,
for
any
k_{0}\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}

and
any smooth function
\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

whose support is included in

\{(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}:1+2\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re[\widehat{m}^{j}e_{-j}(x)]\geq 1/c\}
,

(89)

\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Bigl(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{k_{0}}(m)-\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}^{k_{0}}(m)\Bigr)\varphi\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\biggr|\leq C\delta,

for a constant
C
only depending on
\varphi
through
\|\varphi\|_{\infty}

and
\|\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\varphi\|_{\infty}
.
By an obvious regularization procedure, the same holds if
\varphi
is merely bounded
and
Lipschitz continuous, in which case the constant
C
depends on
\varphi
through
the bound and the Lipschitz constant of
\varphi
.

For
\vartheta:{\mathbb{R}}\rightarrow[0,1]
,
a
c
-Lipschitz function
that is equal to
0
on
]-\infty,1/c[
, let

\Theta\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)=\vartheta\Bigl(1+2\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re\bigl[\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)\bigr]\Bigr),\quad\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}.

Observe that
the support of
\Theta
is included in
{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
.
Moreover,
for
(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}

and

\widehat{r}^{k_{0}}\in{\mathbb{C}}
, with
k_{0}\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}
, we have

\begin{split}&\Bigl|\Theta\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}+{\mathbf{1}_{\{k=k_{0}\}}}\widehat{r}^{k_{0}}\Bigr)-\Theta\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\Bigr|\\
&\leq 2c\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\biggl|\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}+{\mathbf{1}}_{\{k=k_{0}\}}\widehat{r}^{k_{0}}\bigr)e_{-k}(x)\biggr|\leq 2c\bigl|\widehat{r}^{k_{0}}\bigr|,\end{split}

hence proving that
\Theta
is
2c
-Lipschitz
with respect to
\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}
.
In particular, for a continuously differentiable test function
\phi
on
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
,
we deduce from
(
89
)
that, for
N_{0}
large enough,

\begin{split}&\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Bigl(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{k_{0}}(m)-\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}^{k_{0}}(m)\Bigr)\phi\bigl(m*f_{N_{0}}\bigr)\vartheta\Bigl(1+2\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re\bigl[\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)\bigr]\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\biggr|\leq C(c)\delta,\end{split}

with
C(c)
depending on
c
, but being independent of
N_{0}
and
\delta
.
Above,
\phi(m*f_{N_{0}})
is regarded as a smooth function on
{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
.

As before,
m*f_{N_{0}}
converges to
m
for the Wasserstein distance
as
N_{0}
tends to
\infty
.
Moreover, Lemma

6.13

below says that,

{\mathbb{P}}
-almost everywhere, the series
\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}|\widehat{m}^{k}|

is absolutely convergent hence proving that

1+\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}2\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re[\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)]

converges to
\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}m(x)

with
m
being identified with its density.
Therefore, we can let
N_{0}
tend to
\infty
. We thus get

\begin{split}\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Bigl(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{k_{0}}(m)-\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}^{k_{0}}(m)\Bigr)\phi(m)\vartheta\Bigl(\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}m(x)\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)=0.\end{split}

There is no difficulty for sending
c
to
+\infty
in the definition of
\vartheta

since
{\mathbb{P}}
almost every
m\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
has a (strictly) positive density.
We obtain

\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Bigl(\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{k_{0}}(m)-\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}^{k_{0}}(m)\Bigr)\phi(m)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)=0,

for any
continuously differentiable test function
\phi
on
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
.
By the approximation procedure
defined in Proposition

3.14
,
the same holds true if

\phi
is merely continuous on
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
.
And, then
the same is true
if

\phi
is bounded and measurable on
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
, see

[
65
]
.
This suffices to identify

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{k_{0}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}^{k_{0}}
.
∎

5.4.
Classical solutions to the master equation as weak solutions

We now prove the following result, which legitimates Definition

5.1
:

Proposition 5.9
.

If
U
is a classical solution to the master equation,
with
\partial_{t}U
,
\partial_{x}U
,
\partial^{2}_{xx}U
,
\partial_{\mu}U
,
\partial_{y}\partial_{\mu}U

being continuous
(with
y

denoting the last argument in the derivative
\partial_{\mu}U(t,x,m)(y)
), then the centered version

\tilde{U}(t,x,m):=U(t,x,m)-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}U(t,y,m)dy,\quad t\in[0,T],\ x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d},\ m\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d}),

satisfies the
conservative version (
24
) of the master equation.
In particular,
\tilde{U}
is a weak solution to the master equation, in the sense of Definition
5.1
.

Proof.

For any starting point
(t,m)\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
, we can construct
a solution to the system
(
5
) by solving
the Fokker-Planck equation

\partial_{t}m_{t}(x)-\textrm{\rm div}_{x}\Bigl(\partial_{p}H\bigl(x,\nabla_{x}U(t,x,m_{t})\bigr)m_{t}(x)\Bigr)-\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{x}m_{t}(x)=0,

and then, by letting,
u_{t}(x)=U(t,x,m_{t})
.
In fact, this solution can be proven to be unique, see for instance

[
25
, Proposition 5.106]
.

By Proposition

2.6
, the forward component of this unique solution, i.e.
(m_{t})_{0\leq t\leq T}
, must coincide with the (hence unique)
optimal path of the MFCP, when initialized from
(t,m)
.
In turn,
Lemma

4.6

implies, for any
N\geq 1
,

\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k}}V(t,m)=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}U(t,x,m)e^{-2{\mathrm{i}}\pi k\cdot x}dx,\quad k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\},

for almost every
(t,m)\in[0,T] \times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
under the probability measure
\textrm{\rm Leb}_{1}\otimes{\mathbb{P}}_{N}
.
By continuity of
U
in the right-hand side,
we deduce that
V
is differentiable with respect to
\widehat{m}^{k}
on the entire
[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
.
We deduce from Schwarz’ theorem that, for
every
(t,m)\in[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}_{N}

and
for
any

k,\ell\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}
,

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\partial_{\widehat{m}^{\ell}}U(t,x,m)e^{-2{\mathrm{i}}\pi k\cdot x}dx=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k}}U(t,x,m)e^{-2{\mathrm{i}}\pi\ell\cdot x}dx,\quad k,\ \ell\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\},

which rewrites

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,x,m)(y)e^{-2{\mathrm{i}}\pi k\cdot x}e^{-2{\mathrm{i}}\pi\ell\cdot y}dxdy=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,x,m)(y)e^{-2{\mathrm{i}}\pi k\cdot y}e^{-2{\mathrm{i}}\pi\ell\cdot x}dx,

for

k,\ell\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}
.
By invoking the continuity of
U

and by approximating
any measure
m\in\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
by the sequence

(m*f_{N})_{N\geq 1}
,
the above holds true for any
(t,m)\in[0,T]\times\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and any

k,\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
.
This shows

\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,x,m)(y)-\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,y,m)(x)=\psi(y)-\phi(x),\quad x,y\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d},

for two real-valued functions
\phi
and
\psi

defined on
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
(both depending on
m
).
Integrating the above identity in
y
and then in
x
with respect to the Lebesgue measure on
{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
, we get
from
(
3
):

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,y,m)(x)dy=\phi(x)-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\psi(y)dy,\\
&\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,x,m)(y)dx=\psi(y)-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\phi(x)dx,\end{split}

for

x,y\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
.
Integrating again in
x
the first line, we get

\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\psi(y)dy=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\phi(x)dx,

and then

\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,x,m)(y)-\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,y,m)(x)=\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,x,m)(y)dx-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,y,m)(x)dy,

for

x,y\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}
.
We then have

\begin{split}&\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bigg\{\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}H\bigl(y,\nabla_{y}U(t,y,m)\bigr)dm(y)\biggr\}(x)\\
&=H\bigl(x,\nabla_{y}U(t,x,m)\bigr)-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}H\bigl(y,\nabla_{y}U(t,y,m)\bigr)dy\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\partial_{p}H\bigl(y,\nabla_{y}U(t,y,m)\bigr)\cdot\partial_{y}\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,y,m)(x)dm(y)\\
&=H\bigl(x,\nabla_{y}U(t,x,m)\bigr)-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}H\bigl(y,\nabla_{y}U(t,y,m)\bigr)dy\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\partial_{p}H\bigl(y,\nabla_{y}U(t,y,m)\bigr)\cdot\partial_{y}\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,x,m)(y)dm(y)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\partial_{p}H\bigl(y,\nabla_{y}U(t,y,m)\bigr)\cdot\partial_{y}\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,x,m)(y)dx\,dm(y),\end{split}

and similarly,

\begin{split}&\frac{\delta}{\delta m}\bigg\{\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\text{Tr}\Bigl[\partial^{2}_{y}U(t,y,m)\Bigr]dm(y)\bigg\}(x)\\
&=\text{Tr}\Bigl[\partial^{2}_{x}U(t,x,m)\Bigr]-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\text{Tr}\Bigl[\partial^{2}_{y}U(t,y,m)\Bigr]dy\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\text{Tr}\Bigl[\partial^{2}_{y}\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,y,m)(x)\Bigr]dm(y)\\
&=\text{Tr}\Bigl[\partial^{2}_{x}U(t,x,m)\Bigr]-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\text{Tr}\Bigl[\partial^{2}_{y}U(t,y,m)\Bigr]dy\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\text{Tr}\Bigl[\partial^{2}_{y}\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,x,m)(y)\Bigr]dm(y)-\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\text{Tr}\Bigl[\partial^{2}_{y}\frac{\delta U}{\delta m}(t,x,m)(y)\Bigr]dx\,dm(y).\end{split}

The conclusion easily follows: it suffices to
integrate
the equation
(
12
)
in

x
with respect to
the Lebesgue measure, to make the difference between
(
12
)
and the hence integrated version of
(
12
)
and then to insert the latter two identities, noticing that

\nabla_{x}U=\nabla_{x}\tilde{U}
.

The last claim then follows since
\tilde{U}
solves (
76
) in the classical sense. Indeed, we can argue as in Subsection
4.1
, the computations being here legitimate because the solution is smooth.
∎

6.
appendix

6.1.
Construction of a probability measure on
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
satisfying
Theorem
3.5
.

This (long) subsection is dedicated to the proof of
Theorem

3.5
.
This comes as a by-product of a generic construction, which makes use (in a quite systematic manner)
of the notations introduced in Subsection

3.2
(we invite the reader to have a new look at them before she/he enters the details of the proof below).
In particular, we recall
(
27
),
(
28
)
and the subsequent notation

{\mathbb{P}}_{N}=\Gamma_{N}\circ{\mathscr{I}}_{N}^{-1}
.
Quite often in the analysis, we also make use of the normalization constant

(90)

c_{N}:=\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}.

(Notice that
c_{N}
is different from
Z_{N}
in (
27
).)
Moreover, we also introduce a sequence

(\xi_{k})_{k\in{\mathbb{N}}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}

of independent two-dimensional Gaussian random variables on an auxiliary probability space with

{\mathbf{P}}
as probability measure, such that each

\xi_{k}
has
I_{2}

as covariance matrix (with
I_{2}
being the identity matrix of dimension 2).

6.1.1.
Properties of the sequence

({\mathbb{P}}_{N})_{N\geq 1}

In this paragraph, we obtain a series of lemmas on the properties of
the measures

({\mathbb{P}}_{N})_{N\geq 1}
. We start with the following lemma:

Lemma 6.1
.

For any two integers
N_{0}\leq N

and for any
[0,1]
-valued
measurable
function

\varphi
defined on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}
that is equal to zero outside
{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
,

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\leq\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}.\end{split}

Moreover, for any
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,
if

\varphi
is null outside the points

(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}
such that

(91)

1+2\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}2\Re\bigl[\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)\bigr]\geq\varepsilon,

then

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\geq\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}\frac{c(\varepsilon,N_{0})}{Z_{N}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j},\end{split}

with

(92)

c(\varepsilon,N_{0}):=1-\frac{C(p)}{\varepsilon N_{0}^{(p-5/2)d}},

for
C(p)
a constant only depending on
p
and
d
.

Proof.

First Step.

Using the same notations as in the statement and recalling that
\varphi
is zero outside

{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{k}

(93)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 5.0pt\times\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2(|F_{N}^{+}|-|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|)}}\biggl[{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}\biggr]\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}.

Obviously, we can bound the indicator function in the last line by 1 and then get
c_{N}/c_{N_{0}}

(see (
90
) for the definition) as bound for the whole term on the last line.
This proves the first inequality in the statement.

Second Step.

Take now
\varphi
as in the statement and
(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}
satisfying
(
91
).
If we choose another collection
(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}\setminus F_{N_{0}}}

(with the usual requirement that
\widehat{m}^{-k}=\overline{\widehat{m}^{k}}
) such that

\sum_{k\in F_{N}\setminus F_{N_{0}}}|\widehat{m}^{k}|<\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon,

then

1+\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}}\widehat{m}_{k}e_{-k}(x)=1+2\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}}\Re\bigl[\widehat{m}_{k}e_{-k}(x)\bigr]\geq\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon,

and accordingly the left hand side can be regarded as a probability measure in

{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
. For instance, so is the case if

|\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq\kappa_{d}^{-1}\varepsilon|k|^{-3d/2}
, for
\kappa_{d}=2\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\setminus\{0\}}|j|^{-3d/2}
.
Therefore,
for

m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
such that

(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}

belongs to the support of
\varphi
,

(94)

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2(|F_{N}^{+}|-|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|)}}\prod_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{|\widehat{m}^{k}|<\kappa_{d}^{-1}\varepsilon|k|^{-3d/2}\}}\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}\\
&\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2(|F_{N}^{+}|-|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|)}}{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}\\
&\leq\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2(|F_{N}^{+}|-|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|)}}\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}.\end{split}

Multiplying both sides by
c_{N_{0}}/c_{N}
, we get

(95)

\begin{split}&\prod_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}{\mathbf{P}}\biggl(\Bigl\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}|k|^{pd}}|\xi_{k}|<\frac{\varepsilon}{\kappa_{d}|k|^{3d/2}}\Bigr\}\biggr)\\
&\leq\frac{c_{N_{0}}}{c_{N}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2(|F_{N}^{+}|-|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|)}}{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}\\
&\leq 1,\end{split}

where
(\xi_{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}
is defined right below (
90
).

By Markov inequality, for
p>3/2
,

(96)

\begin{split}\prod_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}{\mathbf{P}}\biggl(\Bigl\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}|k|^{pd}}|\xi_{k}|<\frac{\varepsilon}{\kappa_{d}|k|^{3d/2}}\Bigr\}\biggr)&\geq 1-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}{\mathbf{P}}\biggl(\Bigl\{|\xi_{k}|\geq\sqrt{2}\frac{\varepsilon}{\kappa_{d}}|k|^{(p-3/2)d}\Bigr\}\biggr)\\
&\geq 1-C(p)\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\frac{1}{\varepsilon|k|^{(p-3/2)d}}\\
&\geq 1-\frac{C(p)}{\varepsilon N_{0}^{(p-5/2)d}},\end{split}

where the value of the constant
C(p)
is allowed to change from line to line (as long as it only depends on

p
and
d
).
The difference on the last line coincides with

c(\varepsilon,N_{0})
in
(
92
).

Back to
(
95
), we deduce

\begin{split}&c(\varepsilon,N_{0})\leq\frac{c_{N_{0}}}{c_{N}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2(|F_{N}^{+}|-|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|)}}{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}.\end{split}

Back to
(
93
), we get
the second inequality in the statement (and this even if

c(\varepsilon,N_{0})
is negative).
∎

We get the following lemma:

Lemma 6.2
.

With the same notations as above,

\lim_{N_{0}\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\Bigl|\frac{Z_{N_{0}}}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}-1\Bigr|=0,

and

\lim_{N_{0}\rightarrow\infty}\inf_{N\geq N_{0}}{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\biggl(\biggl\{1+\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)\geq(2N_{0})^{-3d/2}\biggr\}\biggr)=1,

with the usual convention that

\widehat{m}^{-k}=\overline{\widehat{m}^{k}}
for
k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}
.

Proof.
