First Step.

We choose

\varphi
in Lemma
6.1
as
\varphi_{0}
, the indicator function of the set
of Fourier coefficients

(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}
such that

(97)

1+\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)>\varepsilon,

for some
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
as in the statement of Lemma
6.1
and
with the same convention
as in the statement of
Lemma

6.2

that

\widehat{m}^{-k}=\overline{\widehat{m}^{k}}
.
Then,
we have

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\varphi_{0}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}\\
&=\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F^{+}_{N_{0}}|}}\varphi_{0}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}.\end{split}

We then make the change of variable

\widehat{y}^{k}=\frac{1}{1-\varepsilon}\widehat{m}^{k}.

Then, the condition
(
97
)
merely says that

(\widehat{y}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}

belongs to
{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
if

(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}
belongs to the support of
\varphi_{0}
.
Therefore,

(98)

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\varphi_{0}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}\\
&=\frac{(1-\varepsilon)^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\exp\biggl(-(1-\varepsilon)^{2}\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{y}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{y}^{j}\\
&\geq(1-\varepsilon)^{2|F^{+}_{N_{0}}|}\frac{Z_{N_{0}}}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}.\end{split}

By the
second inequality in the statement of Lemma
6.1
, we deduce that

c(\varepsilon,N_{0})(1-\varepsilon)^{2|F^{+}_{N_{0}}|}\frac{Z_{N_{0}}}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}\leq 1.

Moreover, choosing
\varphi=1
in
the first inequality in the statement of Lemma
6.1
, we also have

\frac{Z_{N_{0}}}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}\geq 1.

Second Step.

We notice from Proposition

3.1
that
2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|=D_{N_{0}}\leq 2(2N_{0})^{d}
. So,
if we choose

\varepsilon=N_{0}^{-3d/2}
, then
the shape of

c(\varepsilon,N_{0})
in
Lemma
6.1

together with the fact that
p\geq 5

yield

c(\varepsilon,N_{0})(1-\varepsilon)^{2|F^{+}_{N_{0}}|}\geq\Bigl(1-\frac{C(p)}{N_{0}^{d}}\Bigr)\exp\Bigl(2(2N_{0})^{d}\ln(1-(N_{0})^{-3d/2})\Bigr).

Obviously the right-hand side tends to
1
as
N_{0}
tends to
\infty
.
Combining with the conclusion of the first step, we get

\lim_{N_{0}\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\Bigl|\frac{Z_{N_{0}}}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}-1\Bigr|=0,

which is the first claim in the statement.

Third Step.

By the second inequality in the statement of Lemma
6.1
,

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi_{0}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\geq\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}\frac{c(\varepsilon,N_{0})}{Z_{N}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\varphi_{0}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j},\end{split}

By
(
98
) (with
\varepsilon=N_{0}^{-3d/2}
), we get

\begin{split}{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\biggl(\biggl\{1+\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)>N_{0}^{-3d/2}\biggr\}\biggr)&=\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi_{0}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&\geq c(\varepsilon,N_{0})(1-\varepsilon)^{2|F^{+}_{N_{0}}|}\frac{Z_{N_{0}}}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}},\end{split}

By the second step, we get that the infimum over
N\geq N_{0}
of the right-hand side
tends to
1
as
N_{0}
tends to
\infty
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Here is an application, which is very useful throughout the paper.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
(\psi_{N_{0}})_{N_{0}\geq 1}
be a sequence of
[-1,1]
-valued Borel measurable functions defined on

{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
with the following two properties:

(1)

\psi_{N_{0}}
is measurable with respect to the
\sigma
-algebra generated by the mappings

(m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\mapsto\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}
;

(2)

\psi_{N_{0}}
is null outside

A_{N_{0}}:=\biggl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):1+\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)\geq(2N_{0})^{-3d/2}\biggr\}.

Then, there exists
a_{0}>0
, only depending on
d
, such that, for any
a\in(0,a_{0}]
,

\begin{split}&A_{N_{0}}\cap\bigcap_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):|\widehat{m}^{k}|<\frac{a}{|k|^{5d/2}}\Bigr\}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\subset\biggl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):1+\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)\geq 2^{-(3d/2+1)}N_{0}^{-3d/2}\biggr\},\end{split}

and

\begin{split}&\lim_{N_{0}\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\biggl|\biggl(\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\psi_{N_{0}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}(m)\biggr)^{-1}\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\biggl[\psi_{N_{0}}(m)\prod_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{|\widehat{m}^{k}|<a|k|^{-5d/2}\Bigr\}}\biggr]d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)-1\biggr|\\
&=0,\end{split}

with the
numerator in the first line being necessarily equal
0
when
the denominator is
0
and the convention that
the ratio is then understood as
1
.

Remark 6.4
.

In fact, the first claim can be reformulated in the following broader sense.
If
(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}
satisfies, for
N\geq N_{0}
,

1+\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)\geq(2N_{0})^{-3d/2},\quad\textrm{and}\quad\max_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|\widehat{m}^{k}|<\frac{a}{|k|^{5d/2}},

for
a\in(0,a_{0}]
,
then

1+\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)\geq 2^{-(3d/2+1)}N_{0}^{-3d/2}.

In words, there is no need to assume
a priori
that
(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}
are the Fourier coefficients of a probability measure. The conditions on the Fourier coefficients suffice to prove
it
a posteriori
.

Proof.

We first observe that, for

m\in A_{N_{0}}\cap\bigcap_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigl\{|\widehat{m}^{k}|<\frac{a}{|k|^{5d/2}}\Bigr\},

it holds

\begin{split}1+\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)&\geq(2N_{0})^{-3d/2}-\sum_{k\in F_{N}\setminus F_{N_{0}}}\frac{a}{|k|^{5d/2}}\\
&\geq(2N_{0})^{-3d/2}-c_{d}a(N_{0})^{-3d/2},\end{split}

for a constant
c_{d}
only depending on
d
.
Therefore, for
c_{d}a\leq 2^{-(3d/2+1)}
,

\begin{split}1+\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)&\geq 2^{-(3d/2+1)}N_{0}^{-3d/2}.\end{split}

We deduce that, for such an
a
and for
\psi_{N_{0}}
as in the statement,

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\biggl[\psi_{N_{0}}(m)\prod_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{|\widehat{m}^{k}|<a|k|^{-5d/2}\}}\biggr]d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&=\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}}\biggl[\varphi_{N_{0}}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\prod_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{|\widehat{m}^{k}|<a|k|^{-5d/2}\}}\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\biggr]\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j},\end{split}

where

\varphi_{N_{0}}=\psi_{N_{0}}\circ{\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}
, see
the notation in
(
28
).
We rewrite the above equality as

(99)

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\biggl[\psi_{N_{0}}(m)\prod_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{|\widehat{m}^{k}|<a|k|^{-5d/2}\}}\biggr]d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&=\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\varphi_{{N_{0}}}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\times\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2(|F^{+}_{N}|-|F^{+}_{N_{0}}|)}}\prod_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{|\widehat{m}^{k}|<a|k|^{-5d/2}\}}\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}\\
&=\frac{Z_{N_{0}}}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}\biggl(\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\psi_{N_{0}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}(m)\biggr)\prod_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}{\mathbf{P}}\Bigl(\Bigl\{|\xi_{k}|\leq\sqrt{2}a|k|^{(p-5/2)d}\Bigr\}\Bigr).\end{split}

Repeating
(
96
),

\begin{split}\prod_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}{\mathbf{P}}\Bigl(\Bigl\{|\xi_{k}|\leq\sqrt{2}a|k|^{(p-5/2)d}\Bigr\}\Bigr)\geq 1-\frac{C(p)}{aN_{0}^{(p-7/2)d}},\end{split}

for a constant
C(p)
only depending on

p
and
d
.
Therefore, by
Lemma

6.2
, we have

\lim_{N_{0}\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\biggl|\frac{Z_{N_{0}}}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}\prod_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}{\mathbf{P}}\Bigl(\Bigl\{|\xi_{k}|\leq\sqrt{2}a|k|^{(p-5/2)d}\Bigr\}\Bigr)-1\biggr|=0.

Inserting the latter into
(
99
), we get the conclusion.
∎

We deduce the following lemma:

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
a_{0}>0

be as in the statement of Lemma

6.3
. Then,
for any
\delta>0
, there exists

N_{1}\geq 1
such that,
for any
N\geq N_{0}\geq N_{1}
,

(100)

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\Bigl(\bigl\{m>2^{-(3d/2+1)}N_{0}^{-3d/2}\bigr\}\Bigr)\geq{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\biggl(A_{N_{0}}\cap\bigcap_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):|\widehat{m}^{k}|<\frac{a_{0}}{|k|^{5d/2}}\Bigr\}\biggr)\geq 1-\delta,\end{split}

with
A_{N_{0}}

being
as in
Lemma

6.3
.

Proof.

For
\delta
as in the statement, we know
from Lemma

6.3

(with
\psi_{N_{0}}={\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}
)
that we can find

a=a_{0}>0
, only depending on
d
,
such that, for any

N_{0}\geq 1
,

\begin{split}\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\biggl|&{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\biggl(A_{N_{0}}\cap\bigcap_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigl\{|\widehat{m}^{k}|<\frac{a}{|k|^{5d/2}}\Bigr\}\biggr)-{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}\bigl(A_{N_{0}}\bigr)\biggr|\leq\frac{\delta}{2}.\end{split}

By Lemma

6.3
,
for any
N\geq N_{0}
,

\begin{split}&A_{N_{0}}\cap\bigcap_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):|\widehat{m}^{k}|<\frac{a}{|k|^{5d/2}}\Bigr\}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\subset\biggl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):1+\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)\geq 2^{-(3d/2+1)}N_{0}^{-3d/2}\biggr\}.\end{split}

Therefore, for any
N\geq N_{0}
,

(101)

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\biggl(\biggl\{1+\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)\geq 2^{-(3d/2+1)}N_{0}^{-3d/2}\biggr\}\biggr)\\
&\geq{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\biggl(A_{N_{0}}\cap\bigcap_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):|\widehat{m}^{k}|<\frac{a}{|k|^{5d/2}}\Bigr\}\biggr)\\
&\geq{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}\bigl(A_{N_{0}}\bigr)-\tfrac{1}{2}\delta.\end{split}

By Lemma

6.2
, we know that, for
N_{0}
large enough,

{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}\bigl(A_{N_{0}}\bigr)\geq 1-\delta/2
and, then,
the right-hand side is greater than
1-\delta
.
This completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 6.6
.

With the same notations as before,

\lim_{N_{0}\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}^{D_{N_{0}}}\rightarrow[-1,1]}\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d\bigl({\mathbb{P}}_{N}-{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}\bigr)(m)\biggr|=0,

where, for any
N_{0}\geq 1
,
\varphi
in the argument of the supremum is required to be measurable, and with the already used notation

D_{N_{0}}=2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|
.

Proof.

We fix
\delta>0
.
By Lemma

6.5
,
we can choose
a_{0}
as in Lemma
6.3
,
N_{0}
large enough
and
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
small enough
such that, for all
N\geq N_{0}
,

{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\Bigl(\bigl\{m>\varepsilon\bigr\}\Bigr)\geq{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\biggl(A_{N_{0}}\cap\bigcap_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):|\widehat{m}^{k}|<\frac{a_{0}}{|k|^{5d/2}}\Bigr\}\biggr)\geq 1-\delta.

For a function
\varphi
as in the statement, we then have

(102)

\begin{split}&\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&\hskip 30.0pt-\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\biggl({\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}(m)\prod_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{|\widehat{m}^{k}|<a_{0}|k|^{-5d/2}\}}\biggr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\biggr|\leq\delta.\end{split}

We now
apply Lemma

6.3

to the function

m\mapsto\varphi((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}(m)
.
For
N_{0}
large enough, we get

\begin{split}&\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)-\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}(m)\biggr|\leq 3\delta.\end{split}

∎

6.1.2.
Convergence of the sequence
({\mathbb{P}}_{N})_{N\geq 1}

Using the properties proven in the previous paragraph,
we now have all the ingredients to
address the limiting points of the sequence

({\mathbb{P}}_{N})_{N\geq 1}
.

The following lemma proves
the second claim in item
(ii)
of
Theorem
3.5
.

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
{\mathbb{P}}

be a weak limit of
({\mathbb{P}}_{N})_{N\geq 1}
on
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
.
For an integer
N_{0}\geq 1
,
define,
on
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
,
the (sub-probability) measure

{\mathbb{Q}}
by

\frac{d{\mathbb{Q}}}{d{\mathbb{P}}}(m):={\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{P}}_{N_{0}}}(m)={\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr),\quad m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}).

Then, the image of
{\mathbb{Q}}
by the mapping

\pi^{(2)}_{N_{0}}:m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\mapsto\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}

is
supported by

{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}

(i.e.,

{\mathbb{Q}}\circ(\pi^{(2)}_{N_{0}})^{-1}({\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}^{\complement})=0
)
and is
absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure.
Precisely,
for any Borel subset
B
of

{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F^{+}_{N_{0}}|}
that is included
in
{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
,

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{Q}}\circ\bigl(\pi^{(2)}_{N_{0}}\bigr)^{-1}(B)\\
&=\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}{\mathbf{1}}_{B}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\\
&\leq\Bigl(\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\frac{Z_{N_{0}}c_{N}}{Z_{N}c_{N_{0}}}\Bigr)\frac{1}{Z_{N_{0}}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}{\mathbf{1}}_{B}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j},\end{split}

where we recall from
Lemma

6.2
that the first factor in the above right-hand side is finite.

Proof.

Let
E
be an open subset of
{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
(equivalently,
E
is an open subset of

{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F^{+}_{N_{0}}|}
included in
{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
).
We observe that
\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\in E\}
is an open subset
of
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
.

Up to a subsequence, we can assume that

({\mathbb{P}}_{N})_{N\geq 1}

weakly
converges to

{\mathbb{P}}
.
Then,
by Portmanteau theorem,

{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\Bigl\{(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\in E\Bigr\}\Bigr)\leq\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\Bigl(\Bigl\{(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\in E\Bigr\}\Bigr).

Now, by
Lemma
6.1

(with
\varphi={\mathbf{1}}_{E}
), letting
N
tend to
\infty
therein, we get

(103)

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}{\mathbf{1}}_{E}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\leq\Bigl(\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\frac{Z_{N_{0}}c_{N}}{Z_{N}c_{N_{0}}}\Bigr)\frac{1}{Z_{N_{0}}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}{\mathbf{1}}_{E}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j},\end{split}

where we recall from
Lemma

6.2
that the first factor in the above right-hand side is finite.
By definition of
{\mathbb{Q}}
, the left-hand side can be rewritten as

{\mathbb{Q}}\circ(\pi^{(2)}_{N_{0}})^{-1}(E)
.

Take now a Borel subset
B
of

{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F^{+}_{N_{0}}|}
that is included
in
{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
. By outer-regularity of
the Lebesgue measure, we can find, for any
\delta>0
, an open subset
E
of
{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
, containing
B
, such that

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{Z_{N_{0}}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}{\mathbf{1}}_{E}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{Z_{N_{0}}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}{\mathbf{1}}_{B}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}+\delta.\end{split}

By (
103
),

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}{\mathbf{1}}_{E}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\\
&\leq\Bigl(\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\frac{Z_{N_{0}}c_{N}}{Z_{N}c_{N_{0}}}\Bigr)\biggl[\frac{1}{Z_{N_{0}}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}{\mathbf{1}}_{B}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}+\delta\biggr].\end{split}

The left-hand side writes

{\mathbb{Q}}\circ(\pi^{(2)}_{N_{0}})^{-1}(E)
.
This yields

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{Q}}\circ\bigl(\pi^{(2)}_{N_{0}}\bigr)^{-1}(B)\\
&\leq{\mathbb{Q}}\circ\bigl(\pi^{(2)}_{N_{0}}\bigr)^{-1}(E)\\
&\leq\Bigl(\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\frac{Z_{N_{0}}c_{N}}{Z_{N}c_{N_{0}}}\Bigr)\biggl[\frac{1}{Z_{N_{0}}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}{\mathbf{1}}_{B}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}+\delta\biggr].\end{split}

Since this is for any
\delta>0
,
we get the result.
∎

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
{\mathbb{P}}

be a weak limit of
({\mathbb{P}}_{N})_{N\geq 1}
on
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
.
Then

\lim_{N_{0}\rightarrow\infty}{\mathbb{P}}(A_{N_{0}})=1,

with
A_{N_{0}}

being
as in
Lemma

6.3
.

Proof.

By the same argument as in
the proof of
Lemma
6.7
,
A_{N_{0}}
is a closed subset of
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
.
Therefore,
by Portmanteau
theorem (assuming without any loss of generality that

({\mathbb{P}}_{N})_{N\geq 1}
converges to
{\mathbb{P}}
in the weak sense),

{\mathbb{P}}(A_{N_{0}})\geq\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(A_{N_{0}}).

The conclusion follows from Lemma

6.5
.
∎

We now prove item
(iii)
in the statement of Theorem

3.5
.

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
{\mathbb{P}}

be a weak limit of
({\mathbb{P}}_{N})_{N\geq 1}
on
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
.
Then,

\lim_{N_{0}\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}^{{D_{N_{0}}}}\rightarrow[-1,1]}\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d\bigl({\mathbb{P}}-{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}\bigr)(m)\biggr|=0,

where, for any
N_{0}\geq 1
,
\varphi
in the argument of the supremum is required to be measurable,
and with the already used notation

D_{N_{0}}=2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|
.

Proof.

First Step.

Introduce the sequence

\eta_{N_{0}}:=\sup_{\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}^{D_{N_{0}}}\rightarrow[-1,1]}\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d\bigl({\mathbb{P}}_{N}-{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}\bigr)(m)\biggr|,\quad N_{0}\geq 1.

By
Lemma
6.6
,
the sequence
(\eta_{N_{0}})_{N_{0}\geq 1}
converges to
0
.

Take now
\varphi

a continuous function from
{\mathbb{R}}^{D_{N_{0}}}
into
[-1,1]
.
Letting
N
tend to
\infty
,
we get

\begin{split}&\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d\bigl({\mathbb{P}}-{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}\bigr)(m)\biggr|\leq\eta_{N_{0}}.\end{split}

Second Step.

Assume now that
\varphi
is merely measurable and
[-1,1]
-valued.
Consider also another sequence

(\delta_{N_{0}})_{N_{0}\geq 1}
converging to
0
.
Then,
by Lusin’s theorem,
we can find, for each
N_{0}\geq 1
,
a
continuous function
\widetilde{\varphi}
on

{\mathbb{R}}^{D_{N_{0}}}

with values in
[-1,1]
such that

(104)

\displaystyle\Bigl(\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\frac{Z_{N_{0}}c_{N}}{Z_{N}c_{N_{0}}}\Bigr){\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}\Bigl(\Bigl\{\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\not=\widetilde{\varphi}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\Bigr\}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\Bigl(\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\frac{Z_{N_{0}}c_{N}}{Z_{N}c_{N_{0}}}\Bigr)\frac{1}{Z_{N_{0}}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{\varphi\not=\widetilde{\varphi}\}}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}\leq\delta_{N_{0}}.

By Lemma

6.7
,

(105)

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{\varphi\not=\widetilde{\varphi}\}}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\leq\delta_{N_{0}}.\end{split}

Therefore,
by
(
104
)
and
(
105
),

\begin{split}&\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d\bigl({\mathbb{P}}-{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}\bigr)(m)-\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\widetilde{\varphi}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d\bigl({\mathbb{P}}-{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}\bigr)(m)\biggr|\\
&\leq\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Bigl(\varphi-\widetilde{\varphi}\Bigr)\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\biggr|+\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Bigl(\varphi-\widetilde{\varphi}\Bigr)\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}(m)\biggr|\\
&\leq\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Bigl(\varphi-\widetilde{\varphi}\Bigr)\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\biggr|+\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Bigl(\varphi-\widetilde{\varphi}\Bigr)\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}^{\complement}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\biggr|\\
&\hskip 15.0pt+\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Bigl(\varphi-\widetilde{\varphi}\Bigr)\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}(m)\biggr|\\
&\leq 4\delta_{N_{0}}+2{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(A_{N_{0}}^{\complement}\Bigr),\end{split}

where we used
the obvious implication
m\in A_{N_{0}}\Rightarrow(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
(see Lemma

6.3
).
By Lemma

6.8
,
we can modify our choice of
(\delta_{N_{0}})_{N_{0}\geq 1}

such that
the right-hand side is less than
5\delta_{N_{0}}
.

Third Step.

By the first and second steps, we get

\begin{split}&\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d\bigl({\mathbb{P}}-{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}\bigr)(m)\biggr|\leq\eta_{N_{0}}+5\delta_{N_{0}}.\end{split}

The right-hand side tends to
0
as
N_{0}
tends to
\infty
, uniformly with respect to
\varphi
.
This completes the proof.
∎

As a corollary, we deduce

Lemma 6.10
.

The sequence
({\mathbb{P}}_{N})_{N\geq 1}
is weakly converging.

Proof.

Take two weak limits
{\mathbb{P}}
and

{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}
of the sequence

({\mathbb{P}}_{N})_{N\geq 1}
.
By Lemma

6.9
,

(106)

\lim_{N_{0}\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{\varphi:{\mathbb{R}}^{{D_{N_{0}}}}\rightarrow[-1,1]}\biggl|\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d\bigl({\mathbb{P}}-{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}\bigr)(m)\biggr|=0,

where, for any
N_{0}\geq 1
,
\varphi
in the argument of the supremum is required to be measurable.

Therefore,
{\mathbb{P}}
and
{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}
coincide on the
\sigma
-algebra generated by
the mappings

(m\mapsto\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}
, for any
N_{0}\geq 1
.
By Lemma

3.4
together with a standard monotone class argument, we deduce that

{\mathbb{P}}
and
{\mathbb{P}}^{\prime}
coincide.
∎

6.1.3.
Completion of the proof of
Theorem

3.5

We now prove the first claim in item
(ii)
of Theorem

3.5
.

Lemma 6.11
.

Let
{\mathbb{P}}

be the weak limit of
({\mathbb{P}}_{N})_{N\geq 1}
on
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
.
Then,
for any integer
N_{0}\geq 1
,
the image of
{\mathbb{P}}
by the projection mapping

\pi_{N_{0}}^{(1)}:m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\mapsto\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\widehat{f}_{N_{0}}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}

is absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure.

Proof.

For
N_{0}\geq 1
,
we consider a

[0,1]
-valued measurable function

\varphi
defined on

{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F^{+}_{N_{0}}|}
that is equal to
0
outside

{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
.

Following
(
93
), we get, for any
N\geq N_{0}
,

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k}\widehat{f}^{k}_{N_{0}})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&=\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k}\widehat{f}^{k}_{N_{0}})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{k}\\
&=\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k}\widehat{f}^{k}_{N_{0}})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\\
&\hskip 5.0pt\times\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2(|F_{N}^{+}|-|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|)}}\biggl[{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}\biggr]\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}.\end{split}

And then,

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k}\widehat{f}^{k}_{N_{0}})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k}\widehat{f}^{k}_{N_{0}})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{m}^{j}.\end{split}

We make, in the integral on the second line, the following change of variable:

\widehat{y}^{k}=\widehat{m}^{k}\widehat{f}^{k}_{N_{0}},\quad k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}.

Then, since
|\widehat{f}^{k}_{N_{0}}|\leq 1
for each
k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}
, we get

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k}\widehat{f}^{k}_{N_{0}})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}\biggl(\prod_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigl|\widehat{f}^{k}_{N_{0}}|\biggr)^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{y}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\frac{|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{y}^{k}|^{2}}}{|\widehat{f}^{k}_{N_{0}}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{y}^{j}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}\biggl(\prod_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigl|\widehat{f}^{k}_{N_{0}}|\biggr)^{-1}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{y}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}{|\widehat{y}^{k}|^{2}}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{y}^{j}.\end{split}

Since
\varphi
is zero outside
{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
, the above integral reduces to an integral over

{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
.
Therefore,

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k}\widehat{f}^{k}_{N_{0}})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{Z_{N}}\frac{c_{N}}{c_{N_{0}}}\biggl(\prod_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigl|\widehat{f}^{k}_{N_{0}}|\biggr)^{-1}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{y}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{y}^{j}.\end{split}

By Lemma

6.6
, we can let
N
tend to
\infty
in the left-hand side.
We
get

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k}\widehat{f}^{k}_{N_{0}})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\\
&\leq\Bigl(\sup_{N\geq N_{0}}\frac{Z_{N_{0}}c_{N}}{Z_{N}c_{N_{0}}}\Bigr)\frac{1}{Z_{N_{0}}}\biggl(\prod_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigl|\widehat{f}^{k}_{N_{0}}|\biggr)^{-1}\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{y}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}\biggr)\bigotimes_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}d\widehat{y}^{j},\end{split}

where we recall from
Lemma

6.2
that the first factor in the above right-hand side is finite.
This completes the proof.
∎

We now prove item
(i)
in the statement of Theorem

3.5
.

Lemma 6.12
.

Let
{\mathbb{P}}

be the weak limit of
({\mathbb{P}}_{N})_{N\geq 1}
on
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
.
Then,

{\mathbb{P}}

has a full support on
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
equipped with the
1
-Wasserstein distance.

Proof.

First Step.

We consider
a measure
m_{0}\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
and a real
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. We have to prove
that
{\mathbb{P}}(B(m_{0},\varepsilon))>0
with

B(m_{0},\varepsilon)
the ball of

{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
of
center

m_{0}
and of radius
\varepsilon
for the 1-Wasserstein distance.

By Lemma

3.3
, we notice that, for
N_{0}
large enough,

m_{0}*f_{N_{0}}=\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}}\widehat{m}_{0}^{k}\widehat{f}_{N_{0}}^{k}e_{-k}\in B\bigl(m_{0},\tfrac{1}{4}\varepsilon\bigr),

And then,
using the same constant
c_{0}
as in the notation
introduced in Section
1
, we get

(107)

\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}+\bigl(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}\bigr)m_{0}*{f}_{N_{0}}=\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}+\bigl(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}\bigr)\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}}\widehat{m}_{0}^{k}\widehat{f}_{N_{0}}^{k}e_{-k}\in B(m_{0},\tfrac{3}{4}\varepsilon).

Recall that there exists a constant
c_{d}
, only depending on
d
, such that, for any
a>0
,

\sum_{k\not\in F_{N_{0}}}\frac{a}{|k|^{5d/2}}\leq\frac{c_{d}a}{{N_{0}}^{3d/2}}.

Therefore, for

a
fixed, for

c_{d}a/N_{0}^{3d/2}<\varepsilon/(8c_{0})

and
for any
collection
(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}
, with

\widehat{m}^{0}=1
,

\widehat{m}^{-k}=\overline{\widehat{m}^{k}}
for
k\not=0
, and

\begin{cases}&\displaystyle\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}\Bigl|\widehat{m}^{k}-\bigl(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}\bigr)\widehat{m}_{0}^{k}\widehat{f}_{N_{0}}^{k}\Bigr|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8c_{0}},\\
&\displaystyle\forall k\not\in F_{N_{0}},\quad|\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq a|k|^{-5d/2},\end{cases}

it holds that

\begin{split}&\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\Bigl|\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)-\Bigl(\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}+\bigl(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}\bigr)m_{0}*{f}_{N_{0}}(x)\Bigr)\Bigr|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8c_{0}},\\
&\sup_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\Bigl|m(x)-\Bigl(\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}+\bigl(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}\bigr)m_{0}*{f}_{N_{0}}(x)\Bigr)\Bigr|<\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}},\end{split}

with

m=\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}.

Therefore,
from
(
107
),

m\in B(m_{0},\varepsilon)

and

(108)

\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\biggl(\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)\biggr)\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}+\bigl(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}\bigr)\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}m_{0}*{f}_{N_{0}}(x)-\frac{\varepsilon}{8c_{0}}\geq\frac{\varepsilon}{8c_{0}}>c_{d}aN_{0}^{-3d/2}.

From the fact that

m\in B(m_{0},\varepsilon)
,
we deduce that,
in order to complete the proof, it suffices to choose
a
such that, for some
N_{0}
large enough,

(109)

{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\biggl\{\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}\bigl|\widehat{m}^{k}-\bigl(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}\bigr)\widehat{m}_{0}^{k}\widehat{f}_{N_{0}}^{k}\bigr|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8c_{0}}\biggr\}\cap\biggl\{\forall k\not\in F_{N_{0}},\ |\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq a|k|^{-5d/2}\biggr\}\biggr)>0.

Second Step.

We claim that in order to prove (
109
), it suffices to prove that there exists
c>0
such that, for some
N_{0}
large enough and
for
N\geq N_{0}
,

(110)

{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\biggl(\biggl\{\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}\bigl|\widehat{m}^{k}-\bigl(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}\bigr)\widehat{m}_{0}^{k}\widehat{f}_{N_{0}}^{k}\bigr|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8c_{0}}\biggr\}\cap\biggl\{\forall k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+},\ |\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq a|k|^{-5d/2}\biggr\}\biggr)\geq c.

Assume indeed that the above holds true. Then, for a given
N_{1}\geq N_{0}
and for
N\geq N_{1}
,
we have

{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\biggl(\biggl\{\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}\bigl|\widehat{m}^{k}-\bigl(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}\bigr)\widehat{m}_{0}^{k}\widehat{f}_{N_{0}}^{k}\bigr|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8c_{0}}\biggr\}\cap\biggl\{\forall k\in F_{N_{1}}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+},\ |\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq a|k|^{-5d/2}\biggr\}\biggr)\geq c.

Then, by Portmanteau theorem, we can easily replace
{\mathbb{P}}_{N}
by

{\mathbb{P}}
in the left-hand side.
Letting
N_{1}
tend to
\infty
, we then get
(
109
).

We now prove
(
110
).
To do so, we apply Lemma

6.3
.
Assuming that
a
right above is greater than
a_{0}
in the statement of Lemma

6.3
, it suffices to show that

{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\biggl(\biggl\{\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}\bigl|\widehat{m}^{k}-\bigl(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}\bigr)\widehat{m}_{0}^{k}\widehat{f}_{N_{0}}^{k}\bigr|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8c_{0}}\biggr\}\cap\biggl\{\forall k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+},\ |\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq\frac{1}{2}a_{0}|k|^{-5d/2}\biggr\}\biggr)\geq c.

From
(
108
), we also notice that, for
a
large enough (the threshold being independent of
N_{0}
and only depending on
d
),

\biggl\{\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}\bigl|\widehat{m}^{k}-\bigl(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}\bigr)\widehat{m}_{0}^{k}\widehat{f}_{N_{0}}^{k}\bigr|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8c_{0}}\biggr\}\subset A_{N_{0}},

with
A_{N_{0}}
as in the statement of Lemma
6.3
.
Therefore, Lemma

6.3
says that, for some
N_{0}
large enough, for any
N\geq N_{0}
,

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\biggl(\biggl\{\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}\bigl|\widehat{m}^{k}-\bigl(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}\bigr)\widehat{m}_{0}^{k}\widehat{f}_{N_{0}}^{k}\bigr|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8c_{0}}\biggr\}\cap\biggl\{\forall k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+},\ |\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq\frac{1}{2}a_{0}|k|^{-5d/2}\biggr\}\biggr)\\
&\geq\frac{1}{2}{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}\biggl(\biggl\{\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}\bigl|\widehat{m}^{k}-\bigl(1-\frac{\varepsilon}{4c_{0}}\bigr)\widehat{m}_{0}^{k}\widehat{f}_{N_{0}}^{k}\bigr|\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{8c_{0}}\biggr\}\biggr).\end{split}

Obviously,

((1-\varepsilon/(4c_{0}))\widehat{m}_{0}^{k}\widehat{f}_{N_{0}}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}\setminus\{0\}}
is in

{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
since
1+\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}\setminus\{0\}}(1-{\varepsilon}/(4c_{0}))\widehat{m}_{0}^{k}\widehat{f}_{N_{0}}^{k}\geq\varepsilon/(4c_{0})
.
Therefore, using the fact that the density of
{\Gamma}_{N_{0}}

in
(
27
)
is strictly positive on
{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
,
the last term in the above inequality is strictly positive.
∎

It remains to see that

Lemma 6.13
.

Let
{\mathbb{P}}

be the weak limit of
({\mathbb{P}}_{N})_{N\geq 1}
on
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
.
Then, for
{\mathbb{P}}
-almost every
m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
,

m
has a strictly positive density
and
\sum_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}|k||\widehat{m}^{k}|<\infty
.

Proof.

From Lemma
6.5
,
we recall that, with
a_{0}
as therein, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, for
N_{0}
large enough
and for
N\geq N_{1}\geq N_{0}
,

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{P}}_{N}\biggl(A_{N_{0}}\cap\bigcap_{k\in F_{N_{1}}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):|\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq\frac{a_{0}}{|k|^{5d/2}}\Bigr\}\biggr)\geq 1-\delta,\end{split}

where
A_{N_{0}}

is as in the statement of
Lemma

6.3
.
By arguing as in the proof of
Lemma

6.12
, we can apply Portmanteau theorem and replace
{\mathbb{P}}_{N}
by

{\mathbb{P}}

(with
N_{1}
being fixed in the intersection symbol).
Then, letting
N_{1}
tend to
\infty
, we get

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(A_{N_{0}}\cap\bigcap_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):|\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq\frac{a_{0}}{|k|^{5d/2}}\Bigr\}\biggr)\geq 1-\delta,\end{split}

with
F_{\infty}^{+}:=\bigcup_{N\geq 1}F_{N}^{+}
.
In particular, since
\delta
is arbitrary,

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{P}}\biggl(\bigcup_{N_{0}\geq 1}\biggl(A_{N_{0}}\cap\bigcap_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):|\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq\frac{a_{0}}{|k|^{5d/2}}\Bigr\}\biggr)\biggr)=1.\end{split}

Take now
m
in the event that appears in the left-hand side. We can find
N_{0}\geq 1

such that
m
belongs to
A_{N_{0}}
and, for any
N\geq N_{0}
,

|\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq\frac{a_{0}}{|k|^{5d/2}}.

By Lemma

6.3
, this implies

1+\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}}\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}(x)\geq 2^{-(3d/2+1)}N_{0}^{-3d/2}

Given the decay of the Fourier modes of
m
, it is easy to deduce that

m
has a continuously differentiable density (which we identify with
m
itself) and that

\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}m(x)\geq 2^{-(3d/2+1)}N_{0}^{-3d/2},

which completes the proof.
∎

6.2.
Proof of Theorem

3.10
(Rademacher’s theorem on

({\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}),{\mathbb{P}})
)

First part: existence of the derivative

We follow the main lines of the proof given in
[
13
, Theorem 10.6.4]
(with the little difference that the latter is
given for functionals defined on a Banach space, which makes it slightly easier).

We start with the following definition. For an integer
N\geq 1
, we consider the mapping

(111)

{\mathscr{L}}_{N,\infty}:m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\mapsto\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\in\ell^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}(F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}):=\bigl\{(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}:\widehat{r}^{k}\in{\mathbb{C}}\bigr\},

with
F_{\infty}^{+}=\{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}:\sharp(k)>0\}=\bigcup_{n\geq 1}F_{n}^{+}
,
and we denote by
{\textsf{P}}_{N,\infty}
the image (on
\ell^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}(F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+})
) of the measure
{\mathbb{P}}

by

{\mathscr{L}}_{N,\infty}
and, then
by

\Bigl({\mathbb{P}}_{N|N,\infty}\bigl(\,\cdot\,|(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\bigr)\Bigr)_{(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k}\in\ell^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}(F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+})}

a regular conditional probability distribution of
{\mathbb{P}}
given
{\mathscr{L}}_{N,\infty}
, namely,
for any Borel subsets
A
of
{\mathbb{C}}^{|F_{N}^{+}|}(\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|})
and
B
of

\ell^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}(F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+})
, it holds that

(112)

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\bigl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\in A\ \textrm{\rm and}\ (\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\in B\bigr\}\Bigr)\\
&=\int_{\ell^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}(F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+})}\biggl[{\mathbb{P}}_{N|(N,\infty)}\Bigl(\bigl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\in A\bigr\}|(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\\
&\hskip 90.0pt\times{\mathbf{1}}_{B}\Bigl((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\biggr]d{\textsf{P}}_{N,\infty}\Bigl((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr).\end{split}

In particular, recalling
from Lemma

6.13
that,
for

{\mathbb{P}}

almost every

m
,

\sum_{j\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}|\widehat{m}^{j}|<\infty
,
we deduce that,
for

\textsf{\sf P}_{N,\infty}
almost every

(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}
,

\sum_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}|\widehat{r}^{j}|<\infty
.

We now consider the set

C_{N}:=\bigl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):\phi\ \textrm{\rm is differentiable at {m} along the directions}\ (\mathfrak{P}[e_{k}])_{k\in F_{N}^{+},\mathfrak{P}=\Re,\Im}\bigr\}.

In clear,

m\in C_{N}

if,
for any
k\in F_{N}^{+}
,
for
\mathfrak{P}=\Re,\Im
,
the limit

\lim_{\eta\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{\eta}\Bigl(\phi\bigl(m+\eta\mathfrak{P}[e_{k}]\bigr)-\phi(m)\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\bigl(m+\eta\mathfrak{P}[e_{k}]\bigr)

exists, which proves that

C_{N}
is a Borel subset of

{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
.
We now address,
for a given

(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}
in
\ell^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}(F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+})
,
the probability

{\mathbb{P}}_{N|(N,\infty)}(C_{N}|(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}})
. By definition of a regular conditional probability distribution, we have, for

\textsf{\sf P}_{N,\infty}
almost every

(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}
,

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{P}}_{N|(N,\infty)}\bigl(C_{N}|(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\bigr)\\
&={\mathbb{P}}_{N|(N,\infty)}\Bigl(\bigcap_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\bigcap_{\mathfrak{P}=\Re,\Im}\Bigl\{m:\lim_{\eta\rightarrow 0}\frac{1}{\eta}\Bigl(\phi(m+\eta\mathfrak{P}[e_{k}])-\phi(m)\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}(m+\eta\mathfrak{P}[e_{k}])\ \textrm{\rm exists}\Bigr\}\\
&\hskip 75.0pt\cap\bigcap_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\{\widehat{m}^{k}=\widehat{r}^{k}\}\,|\,(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr).\end{split}

Obviously, on the event

\bigcap_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\{\widehat{m}^{k}=\widehat{r}^{k}\}
,
we have, for any
k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}

and for
\mathfrak{P}=\Re,\Im
,

(113)

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\eta}\Bigl(\phi(m+\eta\mathfrak{P}[e_{k}])-\phi(m)\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}(m+\eta\mathfrak{P}[e_{k}])\\
&=\frac{1}{\eta}\biggl\{\phi\Bigl(1+\sum_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{m}^{j}e_{-j}]+\eta\mathfrak{P}[e_{k}]+\sum_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{r}^{j}e_{-j}]\Bigr)\\
&\hskip 30.0pt-\phi\Bigl(1+\sum_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{m}^{j}e_{-j}]+\sum_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{r}^{j}e_{-j}]\Bigr)\biggr\}\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\times{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Bigl(1+\sum_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{m}^{j}e_{-j}]+\eta\mathfrak{P}[e_{k}]+\sum_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{r}^{j}e_{-j}]\Bigr).\end{split}

For
(\widehat{r}^{j})_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}
in
\ell^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}(F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+})
such that
\sum_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}|\widehat{r}^{j}|<\infty
, we let

\begin{split}&{\mathcal{O}}_{N}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{j})_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\bigr)\\
&:=\biggl\{(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{|F_{N}^{+}|}:\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\Bigl[1+\sum_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{m}^{j}e_{-j}(x)]+\sum_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{r}^{j}e_{-j}(x)]\Bigr]>0\biggr\}.\end{split}

It is easy to see that

{\mathcal{O}}_{N}((\widehat{r}^{j})_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}})

is an open convex
subset
of
{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}
(regarding the complex coordinates as pairs of reals).
Its closure
writes

\begin{split}&\overline{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{j})_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\bigr)\\
&:=\biggl\{(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{|F_{N}^{+}|}:\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\Bigl[1+\sum_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{m}^{j}e_{-j}(x)]+\sum_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{r}^{j}e_{-j}(x)]\Bigr]\geq 0\biggr\}.\end{split}

We can consider

p^{N}
the orthogonal projection from

{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}
onto

\overline{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}((\widehat{r}^{j})_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}})
(for simplicity, we do not specify the dependence upon

(\widehat{r}^{j})_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}
in the notation
p^{N}
).
We then let

\begin{split}&\phi^{N}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}|(\widehat{r}^{j})_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\\
&:=\phi\biggl(1+\sum_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}2\Re\Bigl\{\Bigl[p^{N}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}}\Bigr)\Bigr]_{j}e_{-j}\Bigr\}+\sum_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}2\Re[ \widehat{r}^{j}e_{-j}]\Bigr\}\biggr).\end{split}

Obviously,
\phi^{N}
is a Lipschitz function on

{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}
. As such, it is differentiable almost everywhere:
we call
{\mathcal{D}}[\phi^{N}]

the set of differentiability points.
Back to
(
113
), we have, on the event

\bigcap_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\{\widehat{m}^{k}=\widehat{r}^{k}\}
,
the identity

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{\eta}\Bigl(\phi(m+\eta\mathfrak{P}[e_{k}])-\phi(m)\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}(m+\eta\mathfrak{P}[e_{k}])\\
&=\frac{1}{\eta}\Bigl[\phi^{N}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{j}+\eta z_{\mathfrak{P}}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{k=j\}})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}|(\widehat{r}^{j})_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)-\phi^{N}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}|(\widehat{r}^{j})_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)\Bigr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\times{\mathbf{1}}_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Bigl(1+\sum_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{m}^{j}e_{-j}]+\eta\mathfrak{P}[e_{k}]+\sum_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{r}^{j}e_{-j}]\Bigr),\end{split}

with
z_{\mathfrak{P}}=1
if
\mathfrak{P}=\Re
and
z_{\mathfrak{P}}={\mathrm{i}}
if
\mathfrak{P}=\Im
.
In particular, if

(\widehat{m}^{j}+\eta z_{\mathfrak{P}}{\mathbf{1}}_{\{k=j\}})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}

belongs to

{\mathcal{O}}_{N}((\widehat{r}^{j})_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}})
and to
{\mathcal{D}}[\phi^{N}]
, then
the above right-hand side has a limit as
\eta
tends to
0
.
Therefore,
for
\textsf{P}_{N,\infty}
-a.e.
(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}
,

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{P}}_{N|(N,\infty)}\bigl(C_{N}|(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\bigr)\\
&\geq{\mathbb{P}}_{N|(N,\infty)}\Bigl({\mathcal{D}}[\phi^{N}]\cap\Bigl\{(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{N}((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}})\Bigr\}\cap\bigcap_{|k|\geq N}\{\widehat{m}^{k}=\widehat{r}^{k}\}\,|\,(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr).\end{split}

We now prove the following two things:
for
\textsf{P}_{N,\infty}
-a.e.
(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}
,

(114)

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{P}}_{N|(N,\infty)}\Bigl({\mathcal{D}}[\phi^{N}]\,|\,(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)=1,\\
&{\mathbb{P}}_{N|(N,\infty)}\Bigl(\Bigl\{(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{N}((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}})\Bigr\}\cap\bigcap_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\{\widehat{m}^{k}=\widehat{r}^{k}\}\,|\,(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)=1.\end{split}

Thanks to (
112
), this is sufficient to prove

{\mathbb{P}}(C_{N})=1

We start with the proof of the first claim
in (
114
).
We invoke
(ii)
in Theorem

3.5
.
Moreover, we introduce the function

{\mathscr{R}}^{N}:(\widehat{\varrho}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{|F_{N}^{+}|}\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}\mapsto(\widehat{\varrho}^{j}\widehat{f}_{N}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}
.
Since

\widehat{f}_{N}^{j}
is a (strictly) positive real for each
j\in F_{N}^{+}
,

{\mathscr{R}}^{N}
can be identified with a linear mapping
from
{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}

into itself
with a (strictly) positive determinant. In particular,

(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}
belongs to

{\mathcal{D}}[\phi^{N}]
if and only if

{\mathscr{R}}^{N}((\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}})
belongs to

{\mathscr{R}}^{N}({\mathcal{D}}[\phi^{N}])

and the complementary of the latter has a zero Lebesgue measure. Here now comes
Theorem

3.5
:
for
m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
,

{\mathscr{R}}^{N}((\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}})

rewrites

\pi_{N}^{(1)}(m)
,
and the image of
the probability measure

{\mathbb{P}}
by
\pi_{N}^{(1)}
is absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure on
{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}
, from which we deduce that

{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\Bigl\{m:(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\in{\mathcal{D}}[\phi^{N}]\Bigr\}\Bigr)={\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\Bigl\{m:\pi_{N}^{(1)}(m)\in{\mathscr{R}}^{N}({\mathcal{D}}[\phi^{N}])\Bigr\}\Bigr)=1.

In turn, we get
that the first line in
(
114
)
holds true for

\textsf{P}_{N,\infty}
almost every
(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}
.

We proceed similarly for the second claim in
(
113
).
We know that

{\mathbb{P}}\Bigl(\Bigl\{m:\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}m(x)>0\Bigr\}\Bigr)=1.

Therefore,
for
\textsf{P}_{N,\infty}
almost every
(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}
,

\begin{split}&{\mathbb{P}}_{N|(N,\infty)}\Bigl(\Bigl\{m:\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\Bigl[1+\sum_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{m}^{j}e_{-j}(x)]+\sum_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{r}^{j}e_{-j}(x)]\Bigr]>0\Bigr\}\\
&\hskip 150.0pt\cap\bigcap_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\{\widehat{m}^{k}=\widehat{r}^{k}\}\,|\,(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)=1,\end{split}

from which we easily deduce that the second line in
(
114
)
is indeed satisfied.

Notice from
(
113
)
that, for any
k_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}
and any
N_{0}\geq|k_{0}|
, the partial derivative
\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\phi(m)
, which exists
for

{\mathbb{P}}
almost every

m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
,
coincides with
the finite dimensional derivative

\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\phi(1+\sum_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{m}^{j}e_{-j}]+\sum_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}2\Re[\widehat{r}^{j}e_{-j}])

on the event
\cap_{j\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\{\widehat{m}^{j}=\widehat{r}^{j}\}
.

Second part: identification of the derivative with a weak limit

We now turn to the proof of
(
33
).
Generally speaking,
it relies on Lemmas

5.7

and

5.8
.
It also requires a preliminary result: in order to proceed,
we need to identify, for a given integer
N_{0}\geq 1
, the conditional measure

\textsf{\sf P}_{N_{0},\infty}

introduced in the first part, see
(
111
). Here, we do so, but
on the set

B_{N_{0},\infty}:=\Bigl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):\forall k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+},\ |\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq\frac{a_{0}}{2|k|^{5d/2}}\Bigr\},

with

a_{0}
as in the statement of
Lemma

6.3
.
The complete identification is given (and proven) in
Lemma

6.14

below.
In this second part,
we take it
for granted and explain how
it applies to
the current problem.

Following
the same notations as in
Lemma

5.7
,
we consider a smooth function
\vartheta:{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

whose support is included in

\biggl\{(\widehat{m}^{j})_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}\simeq{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}:1+2\inf_{x\in{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\sum_{j\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re[\widehat{m}^{j}e_{-j}(x)]\geq\frac{1}{c}\biggr\},

for some
c>1
.
In this framework,
Lemma

6.14

says that

(115)

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\phi(m)\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\vartheta\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}\cap B_{N_{0},\infty}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)=\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}(A_{N_{0}})}\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Psi(m){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}(m),\end{split}

where
A_{N_{0}}
is defined
as in Lemma

6.3

and has a (strictly) positive probability for
N_{0}
large enough, see
Lemma
6.8
, and where

\displaystyle\Psi(m)

\displaystyle={\mathbf{1}}_{B_{N_{0},\infty}}(m)\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\biggl[\phi\Bigl({\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{k}-\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr)+m\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt\times\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\vartheta\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\biggr]d\Gamma_{N_{0}}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)

(116)

\displaystyle={\mathbf{1}}_{B_{N_{0},\infty}}(m)\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\biggl[\phi\Bigl({\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{k}-\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr)+m\Bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt\times\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\vartheta\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\biggr]d\Gamma_{N_{0}}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)+\epsilon_{N_{0}},

where
\epsilon_{N_{0}}
tends to
0
as
N_{0}
tends to
\infty
, uniformly with respect to

\|\phi\|_{\infty}
and
\|\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\vartheta\|_{\infty}
.
Importantly,
this identity requires some care:

(a)
First,
the notation

{\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}((\widehat{r}^{k}-\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}})
is rather abusive since

(\widehat{r}^{k}-\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}
may not belong to

{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}
. However,
the definition of

{\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}
easily extends to the entire

{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}
, see (
28
).

(b)

Second,
the notation

{\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}})
is also abusive. Instead,
we should write

{\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}\circ{\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}})
, but this would be obviously heavier.

(c)

Third,
we recall that
the support of
\vartheta

is included
in
A_{N_{0}}
(up
to the embedding

{\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}
and for
N_{0}
large enough).

As a result of the latter observation,
we notice that,
whenever

(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}
belongs to the support of

\vartheta
and

(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}

satisfies

|\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq(a_{0}/2)|k|^{-5d/2}

(which is the case if
the

(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}
’s are the Fourier coefficients of some
m\in B_{N_{0},\infty}
), the distribution

1+2\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}e_{-k}\bigr)+2\sum_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}\bigr)

is a probability measure, see Lemma

6.3
again. Obviously, this probability measure identifies with

{\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}((\widehat{r}^{k}-\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}^{+}}})+m
, which is
the argument of
\phi

in
(
116
),
when the

(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}
’s are the Fourier coefficients of some
m\in B_{N_{0},\infty}
. In particular,
the integrand
in (
116
)
is always well-defined under the sole assumption that

m\in B_{N_{0},\infty}
.
Equivalently, we can regard
\Psi
as a function of

(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}
provided that

|\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq(a_{0}/2)|k|^{-5d/2}
for any

k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}
.
In order to make this clear, we let

(117)

\begin{split}\widetilde{\Psi}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)&:=\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\biggl[\phi\Bigl(1+2\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}e_{-k}\bigr)+2\sum_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}\bigr)\Bigr)\\
&\hskip 125.0pt\times\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\vartheta\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\biggr]d\Gamma_{N_{0}}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr),\end{split}

for

(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+}}\in\ell^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}(F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+})
such that

|\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq(a_{0}/2)|k|^{-5d/2}
for any

k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}
.

And then, by combining
(
115
),
(
116
)
and
(
117
),
we obtain

(118)

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\phi(m)\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\vartheta\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}(m){\mathbf{1}}_{B_{N_{0},\infty}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\\
&=\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}(A_{N_{0}})}\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Psi(m)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)+\epsilon_{N_{0}}\\
&=\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}(A_{N_{0}})}\int_{\ell^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}(F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+})}\widetilde{\Psi}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d\textsf{\sf P}_{N_{0},\infty}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)+\epsilon_{N_{0}},\end{split}

where, as before,
\epsilon_{N_{0}}
tends to
0
as
N_{0}
tends to
\infty
, uniformly in
\|\phi\|_{\infty}
and
\|\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\vartheta\|_{\infty}
.

Now, by the first part of the proof, we know that,
for
\textsf{\sf P}_{N_{0},\infty}
-almost every

(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\in\ell^{2}_{\mathbb{C}}(F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N}^{+})
,
the function

(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}\mapsto\phi\biggl(1+2\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re(\widehat{r}^{k}e_{-k})+2\sum_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re(\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k})\biggr)

is

\textrm{\rm Leb}_{N_{0}}

almost everywhere differentiable along any direction

k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}
. Recalling the form of
\Gamma_{N_{0}}
in
(
27
), we can make an integration by parts in
(
117
). We get:

\begin{split}\widetilde{\Psi}\Bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)&=-\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\biggl[\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\phi\Bigl(1+2\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}e_{-k}\bigr)+2\sum_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}\bigr)\Bigr)\\
&\hskip 25.0pt-2|k_{0}|^{2pd}\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}\phi\Bigl(1+2\sum_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}e_{-k}\bigr)+2\sum_{k\in F_{\infty}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Re\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}e_{-k}\bigr)\Bigr)\biggr]\\
&\hskip 100.0pt\times\vartheta\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d\Gamma_{N_{0}}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr).\end{split}

Then,
we can revert back the computations in
(
116
)
and write

\begin{split}\Psi(m)&=-{\mathbf{1}}_{B_{N_{0},\infty}}(m)\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\biggl[\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\phi\Bigl({\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{k}-\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr)+m\Bigr)\\
&\hskip 100.0pt-2|k_{0}|^{2pd}\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}\phi\Bigl({\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{k}-\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr)+m\Bigr)\biggr]\\
&\hskip 100.0pt\times{\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}^{+}}}\bigr)\vartheta\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d\Gamma_{N_{0}}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)+\epsilon_{N_{0}}.\end{split}

Back to
(
115
)
and
(
118
), we get

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\phi(m)\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\vartheta\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}\cap B_{N_{0},\infty}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\\
&=-\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Bigl[\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\phi(m)-2|k_{0}|^{2pd}\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}\phi(m)\Bigr]\vartheta\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}\cap B_{N_{0},\infty}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)+\epsilon_{N_{0}}.\end{split}

By Lemma

6.5
, we have

{\mathbb{P}}(A_{N_{0}}\cap B_{N_{0},\infty})\rightarrow 1

as
N_{0}\rightarrow\infty
,
from which we deduce that

(119)

\begin{split}&\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\phi(m)\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\vartheta\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)\\
&=-\int_{{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})}\Bigl[\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\phi(m)-2|k_{0}|^{2pd}\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}\phi(m)\Bigr]\vartheta\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)+\epsilon_{N_{0}},\end{split}

where, as before,
\epsilon_{N_{0}}
tends to
0
as
N_{0}
tends to
\infty
, uniformly in
\|\phi\|_{\infty}
,

\|\vartheta\|_{\infty}
,
\|\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\phi\|_{\infty}
and
\|\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\vartheta\|_{\infty}
.

For each

k_{0}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}\setminus\{0\}
, we now call
\widehat{\Phi}^{k_{0}}(m)
a weak limit of the sequence

(\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\phi(m*f_{N}))_{N>|k_{0}|}
with

\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\phi
being here understood, for each
N>|k_{0}|
,
as the almost everywhere (for the Lebesgue measure on
{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
)
derivative of
\phi
on
{\mathcal{P}}_{N}
.
Then,
by combining
Lemma

5.7

and
(
119
), we reach the same conclusion as
(
89
)
in the proof of
Lemma

5.8
, but with

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{1}^{k_{0}}(m)

therein being replaced by

\widehat{\Phi}^{k_{0}}(m)

and
with

\widehat{\mathcal{W}}_{2}^{k_{0}}(m)

being replaced by

\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\phi(m)
(as given by the conclusion of the first part).
We hence conclude that,
almost everywhere under
{\mathbb{P}}
,

\widehat{\Phi}^{k_{0}}

and

\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\phi

are equal, which proves that

(\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\phi(m*f_{N}))_{N>|k_{0}|}

converges (in the weak sense) to

\partial_{\widehat{m}^{k_{0}}}\phi
. ∎

Third part: auxiliary statement

It now
remains to prove the following lemma, which we invoked in the derivation of the above second part.

Lemma 6.14
.

For any bounded measurable function

\varphi:{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
,

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\varphi(m){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}\cap B_{N_{0},\infty}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)=\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}(A_{N_{0}})}\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\psi\bigl(m\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}(m),\end{split}

with
A_{N_{0}}
in the first line being as in Lemma

6.3

and with

\begin{split}&\psi(m)={\mathbf{1}}_{B_{N_{0},\infty}}(m)\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\biggl[\varphi\Bigl({\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr)-{\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}\bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr)+m\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}\circ{\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr)\biggr]\\
&\hskip 250.0pt\times d\Gamma_{N_{0}}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr),\end{split}

and with the same notation
as in (
28
) for the map
{\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}
.

Proof.

Throughout the proof, we use the same abuse of notations as
in the proof of
Theorem

3.10
: We
write

\begin{split}&{\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{k}-\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr)\quad\textrm{\rm for}\quad{\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr)-{\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}\bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr),\\
&\vartheta\bigl((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\bigr)\quad\textrm{\rm for}\quad\vartheta\circ{\mathscr{I}}_{N}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\bigr),\end{split}

when
\vartheta
is defined on

\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
and

(\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{N}
.

First Step.

We start with the following observation, very similar to
an argument used in the proof of Theorem

3.10
.
If, for
N\geq N_{0}
being fixed,
we set

B_{N_{0},N}:=\Bigl\{m\in{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d}):\forall k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+},\ |\widehat{m}^{k}|\leq\frac{a_{0}}{2|k|^{5d/2}}\Bigr\},

then, for any bounded measurable function

\varphi:\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})\rightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
, we have

(120)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\varphi\bigl(m\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}\cap B_{N_{0},N}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N}}\varphi\bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}\bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{B_{N_{0},N}}\bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\bigr)d\Gamma_{N}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle=\frac{Z_{N_{0}}}{Z_{N}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}}\biggl[\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\varphi\bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}}\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}\bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr)d\Gamma_{N_{0}}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\biggr]

\displaystyle\hskip 15.0pt\times{\mathbf{1}}_{B_{N_{0},N}}\biggl((0)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}},(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\biggr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}\biggr)\bigotimes_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}} d\widehat{m}^{k},

where we used Lemma

6.3

in order to derive the last equality together with the fact that

{\mathbf{1}}_{B_{N_{0},N}}((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}})={\mathbf{1}}_{B_{N_{0},N}}((0)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}},(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}})

only depends on

(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}
.

In particular, if we let

(121)

\begin{split}&\psi(m):=\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\varphi\Bigl((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}},(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\bigr)d\Gamma_{N_{0}}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr),\end{split}

for

m\in B_{N_{0},N}

and
\psi(m):=0
otherwise,
then, by Fubini’s theorem,
\psi
is
measurable with respect to the sigma-field generated by
the
mappings

(m\mapsto\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}

and
(
120
)
becomes

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\varphi\bigl(m\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}\cap B_{N_{0},N}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)

(122)

\displaystyle=\frac{Z_{N_{0}}}{Z_{N}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}}\biggl[\Bigl(\psi{\mathbf{1}}_{B_{N_{0},N}}\Bigr)\biggl((0)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}},(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\biggr)

\displaystyle\hskip 150.0pt\times\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}\biggr)\biggr]\bigotimes_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}} d\widehat{m}^{k}.

Notice that
the indicator function

{\mathbf{1}}_{B_{N_{0},N}}
in the product

\psi{\mathbf{1}}_{B_{N_{0},N}}
is redundant with the definition of

\psi
, but we keep it for clarity.

Now, the equality (
120
), with
\varphi
being replaced by
\psi
, leads to

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\psi\bigl(m\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}\cap B_{N_{0},N}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)\\
&=\frac{Z_{N_{0}}}{Z_{N}}\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}}\biggl[\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}{\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}\bigl((\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}^{+}}}\bigr)d\Gamma_{N_{0}}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{m}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\biggr]\\
&\hskip 15.0pt\times\Bigl(\psi{\mathbf{1}}_{B_{N_{0},N}}\Bigr)\Bigl((0)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}},(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}\biggr)\bigotimes_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}} d\widehat{m}^{k}\\
&={\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}(A_{N_{0}})\frac{Z_{N_{0}}}{Z_{N}}\times\int_{{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}|}}\biggl[\Bigl(\psi{\mathbf{1}}_{B_{N_{0},N}}\Bigr)\Bigl((0)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}},(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr)\\
&\hskip 150.0pt\times\exp\biggl(-\sum_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}|k|^{2pd}|\widehat{m}^{k}|^{2}\biggr)\biggr]\bigotimes_{k\in F_{N}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}} d\widehat{m}^{k},\end{split}

and then,
(
122
)
becomes

(123)

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\varphi\bigl(m\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}\cap B_{N_{0},N}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m)=\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}(A_{N_{0}})}\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\psi\bigl(m\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}\cap B_{N_{0},N}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}_{N}(m),\end{split}

where we recall from
Lemma

6.5
, that
for
N_{0}
large enough,

{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}(A_{N_{0}})>0
.

Second Step.

We now send
N\rightarrow\infty
in
(
123
).
In order to do so,
we first assume that

\varphi
is
measurable with respect to
(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{1}}^{+}}

for
N_{1}\geq N_{0}
.
Then, for
N\geq N_{1}
,

\psi
in
(
121
)
only depends, on the set
B_{N_{0},N}
, on
m
through

(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{1}}^{+}\setminus F_{N_{0}}^{+}}
and is independent of
N
.
Next, by using
Lemma
6.9
, we get

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\varphi\bigl(m\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}\cap B_{N_{0},N}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)=\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}(A_{N_{0}})}\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\psi\bigl(m\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}\cap B_{N_{0},N}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)+\varepsilon_{N},\end{split}

where
\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\varepsilon_{N}=0
.
It then remains to observe that
the subsets
(B_{N_{0},N})_{N\geq N_{0}}
are non-increasing and their intersection is precisely
B_{N_{0},\infty}
. Then,
splitting
\varphi
into the difference
\varphi_{+}-\varphi_{-}
, we can easily let
N
tend to
\infty
by regarding the left-hand side and the first term in the right-hand side as the masses of

B_{N_{0},N}
under finite positive measures.
We get

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\varphi\bigl(m\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}\cap B_{N_{0},\infty}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}(m)=\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}_{N_{0}}(A_{N_{0}})}\int_{\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})}\psi\bigl(m\bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}\cap B_{N_{0},\infty}}(m)d{\mathbb{P}}(m),\end{split}

where, on the set

B_{N_{0},\infty}
, we have

\begin{split}&\psi(m)=\int_{{\mathcal{O}}_{N_{0}}}\biggl[\varphi\Bigl({\mathscr{I}}_{N_{0}}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{k}-\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}^{+}}}\bigr)+m\Bigr){\mathbf{1}}_{A_{N_{0}}}\bigl((\widehat{r}^{k})_{k\in F_{N_{0}^{+}}}\bigr)\biggr]d\Gamma_{N_{0}}\Bigl(\bigl(\widehat{r}^{k}\bigr)_{k\in F_{N_{0}}^{+}}\Bigr).\end{split}

By monotone class theorem (together with Lemma
3.4
), we easily extend the result to bounded and measurable functions

\varphi
on
\mathcal{P}(\mathbb{T}^{d})
.

∎

6.3.
Mollification of McKean-Vlasov equations

The following statement plays an important role in the proof of
Theorem

4.12
.

Theorem 6.15
.

Let
W_{1}
and
W_{2}
be two Lipschitz continuous functions on
[0,T]\times{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
,
with
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
being equipped with
d_{-2}
. Then, for a constant

c>1
, we can find
an integer
N_{c}
such that, for
N\geq N_{c}
, for
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and
\rho
as in
Definition

3.12
, the Fokker-Planck equation

(124)

\partial_{t}m_{t}(x)-\textrm{\rm div}\Bigl(D{\mathcal{H}}^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}(t,m_{t})(x)\bigl(m_{t}*f_{N}\bigr)(x)\Bigr)-\frac{1}{2}\Delta m_{t}(x)=0,

for
t\in[0,T]
,
with

\begin{split}&D{\mathcal{H}}_{\lambda}^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}(t,m)(x):=\partial_{p}H\Bigl(x,\lambda\partial_{\mu}W_{1}^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}(t,m)(x)+(1-\lambda)\partial_{\mu}W_{2}^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}(t,m)(x)\Bigr),\\
&D{\mathcal{H}}^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}(t,m)(x):=\int_{0}^{1}D{\mathcal{H}}_{\lambda}^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}(t,m)(x)d\lambda,\end{split}

and with

m_{0}\in B_{N}(c)
as initial condition,
has a (unique) smooth solution with values in
{\mathcal{P}}({\mathbb{T}}^{d})
.
It satisfies

m_{t}\geq 1/c^{\prime},\quad\|\nabla m_{t}\|_{\infty}\leq c^{\prime},\quad t\in[0,T],

for
c^{\prime}>1
independent of
N
.

Proof.

We first recall that the function
(t,m,x)\mapsto D{\mathcal{H}}^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}(t,m)(x)
is smooth. It depends on

m
through
(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}}
. We can extend the mapping
to inputs
m
which are general distributions. It suffices to project the Fourier coefficients on the closure of
{\mathcal{O}}_{N}
.
In short, we call
\Pi_{N}
the mapping that maps
m=(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{d}}
onto

\Pi_{N}(m)
defined by

\widehat{\Pi_{N}(m)}^{k}=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\quad\textrm{\rm if}\ k=0\\
{\pi_{N}\bigl(({\widehat{m}^{j}})_{j\in F_{N}^{+}}\bigr)}^{k}&\quad\textrm{\rm if}\ k\in F_{N}^{+}\\
\overline{\widehat{\Pi_{N}(m)}^{k}}&\quad\textrm{\rm if}\ -k\in F_{N}^{+}\\
0&\quad\textrm{\rm if}\ k\not\in F_{N}\end{array}\right.,

where
\pi_{N}
is the orthogonal projection from
{\mathbb{R}}^{2|F_{N}^{+}|}\simeq{\mathbb{C}}^{|F_{N}^{+}|}
onto
the closure of

{\mathcal{O}}_{N}
.
We then consider

(t,m,x)\mapsto D{\mathcal{H}}^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}(t,\Pi_{N}(m))(x)
. It is Lipschitz
with respect to the Fourier coefficients
(\widehat{m}^{k})_{k\in F_{N}\setminus\{0\}}
.
There is no difficulty for proving that the solution is classical. However,
the solution to the hence extended version of
(
124
) may not take values in
{\mathcal{O}}_{N}
.

We now consider the local Fokker-Planck equation

(125)

\partial_{t}\widetilde{m}_{t}(x)-\textrm{\rm div}\Bigl(D{\mathcal{H}}^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}(t,m_{t})(x)\widetilde{m}_{t}(x)\Bigr)-\frac{1}{2}\Delta\widetilde{m}_{t}(x)=0,

with

\widetilde{m}_{0}=m_{0}\in B_{N}(c)
as initial condition. It is important to notice that, in the non-linear dependence, the argument is

m_{t}
and not
\widetilde{m}_{t}
.
Since
W_{1}
and
W_{2}
are
d_{-2}
-Lipschitz continuous in the measure argument, the field
x\mapsto D{\mathcal{H}}^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}(t,m_{t})(x)

is bounded and Lipschitz continuous, with a bound and a Lipschitz constant that are independent of
N
,
\varepsilon
,
\rho
: it suffices to combine Corollary

3.16

and
the second claim in
Proposition

4.10
.
In turn, the solution to the Fokker-Planck equation satisfies

\widetilde{m}_{t}\geq 1/c^{\prime},\quad\|\nabla_{x}\widetilde{m}_{t}\|_{\infty},\quad\vvvert\nabla_{x}\widetilde{m}_{t}\vvvert_{\gamma}\leq c^{\prime},\quad t\in[0,T],

for a constant
c^{\prime}
that depends on
c
but that is independent of
N
,
\varepsilon
and
\rho
,
and for some
\gamma\in(0,1)
that is also independent of
N
,
\varepsilon
and
\rho
.

We then compare
m
and
\widetilde{m}
.
Denoting by
p(t,x)
the standard heat kernel on the torus,
we have

\begin{split}m_{t}(x)-\widetilde{m}_{t}(x)&=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\nabla_{x}p(t-s,x-y)D{\mathcal{H}}^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}(s,m_{s})(y)\bigl(f_{N}*m(s,y)-\widetilde{m}(s,y)\bigr)dyds,\end{split}

from which we deduce that

\begin{split}\|m_{t}-\widetilde{m}_{t}\|_{\infty}&\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}\frac{\|m_{s}-\widetilde{m}_{s}\|_{\infty}}{\sqrt{t-s}}ds+\eta_{N},\quad t\in[0,T],\end{split}

for a sequence
(\eta_{N})_{N\geq 1}
that tends to
0
as
N
tends to
\infty
and that only depends on
m_{0}
through the value of
c
.
It is standard to deduce that

\|m_{t}-\widetilde{m}_{t}\|_{\infty}\leq C\eta_{N},

which proves that
m_{t}
is positive for
N
large enough. Since
\int_{{\mathbb{T}}^{d}}m_{t}(x)dx=1
, it is a probability measure.

Using the regularity in
y
of

D{\mathcal{H}}^{N,\varepsilon,\rho}(s,y)
together with the Hölder continuity of
\nabla_{x}\widetilde{m}_{t}
, we can proceed in the same way for

\|\nabla_{x}m_{t}-\nabla_{x}\widetilde{m}_{t}\|_{\infty}
.
∎
